Computer Science Exit Exam Study Guide · 233 questions

Read the question first. Select the eye beside the reading hint, study the cited module section, then reveal the answer. Each eye controls only its own block. All hints and answers start closed whenever this page opens.

Read the linked study-guide sections before revealing the answer. PDF page references count from the first page of the document.

233 of 233 questions

Question 1

Which mutual exclusion mechanism keeps track of turns to enter the critical region using an integer variable, which is initially-0, and another process waits until it turns to 1?

  • A. Lock variable
  • B. Disabling interrupts
  • C. Strict alteration
  • D. Race condition

Source

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · 2016 · Question 23

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Track who may enter and distinguish separate tests and updates from atomic operations.

Study-guide sections

OS12 · Turn variables and atomic primitives

OS10 · Races, critical sections and correctness

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS12 · Turn variables and atomic primitives

OS10 · Races, critical sections and correctness

Correct answer: C — Strict alternation (option misspells it as alteration).

OS12 defines strict alternation using turn=0/1: each process waits until turn equals its identifier and passes the turn on leaving. That directly matches the described integer ownership variable. A naive lock variable instead records availability and can race during separate test/set operations. Disabling interrupts and a race condition do not implement the described turn-taking algorithm.

Evidence in the complete study note

Strict alternation uses turn=0/1: process i waits while turn≠i, then enters and sets turn to the other process on leaving. It enforces turns but can violate progress if the other process stays outside its critical region. A naive lock-variable test followed by a separate set races because both processes can observe free before either sets it.OS12
Test-and-set and compare-and-swap combine checking/updating atomically; ordinary check-then-write does not. Peterson’s classic algorithm uses flags plus turn for two processes under its memory-model assumptions; a semaphore can coordinate more than two. Disabling local interrupts does not exclude other CPUs and is not a general user-space locking solution.OS12
A race occurs when results depend on uncoordinated interleaving of conflicting accesses.OS10

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A naive lock variable records free/busy status rather than assigning alternating ownership to process 0 or 1.

B · incorrect — Disabling interrupts does not implement the integer turn-owner protocol described in the stem.

C · correct — Strict alternation uses a turn variable initialized to one process and passed to the other.

D · incorrect — A race condition is a concurrency failure, not a mechanism providing the described alternating access.

Question 2

Which one of the following messaging communication best explains the statement: "Until the message is received, the sending process is blocked'"?

  • A. Blocking receive
  • B. Non-block receive
  • C. Non-block sending
  • D. Blocking send

Source

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · 2016 · Question 31

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify how data moves, which participant waits and what event allows the operation to continue.

Study-guide sections

OS09 · IPC and blocking semantics

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS09 · IPC and blocking semantics

Correct answer: D — Blocking send.

OS09 explicitly identifies a sender blocked until receipt as blocking send under rendezvous semantics. The stem describes the sending process waiting, not a receiver awaiting an incoming message. Nonblocking sending returns promptly rather than waiting for receipt, and receive modes describe the other side. The receipt condition here supplies the relevant API assumption.

Evidence in the complete study note

Blocking send waits for the required handoff/buffering/receipt condition defined by its API. Blocking receive waits for a message. Nonblocking operations return promptly with data, progress or a would-block result. “Sender blocked until received” identifies blocking send under rendezvous semantics.OS09

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Blocking receive waits on the receiving side, whereas the stem explicitly blocks the sender.

B · incorrect — Nonblocking receive returns promptly on the receiving side and does not describe sender waiting.

C · incorrect — Nonblocking sending returns promptly rather than keeping the sender blocked until the handoff completes.

D · correct — Under the stated rendezvous condition, waiting until message receipt is blocking-send behavior.

Question 3

_______is the component of the operating system that is responsible to decide which process to run first among ready processes.

  • A. Memory manager
  • B. Inter process communication
  • C. Disk manager
  • D. Scheduler

Source

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · 2016 · Question 34

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Decide whether the process is eligible, running or waiting, then identify the responsible queue or scheduling role.

Study-guide sections

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

OS01 · Objectives, resource management and evolution

OS09 · IPC and blocking semantics

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

OS01 · Objectives, resource management and evolution

OS09 · IPC and blocking semantics

Correct answer: D — Scheduler

The CPU scheduler selects among ready processes and allocates the CPU (PDF p20). This is precisely the selection task in the stem.

Evidence in the complete study note

The ready queue contains eligible work; device queues hold blocked requests. The broad textbook job queue contains processes in the system, while an admission queue more narrowly holds jobs awaiting memory/admission. Long-term scheduling admits jobs; medium-term scheduling suspends/swaps them; short-term scheduling selects ready work. The dispatcher performs the handoff/context restore after selection. A bare “priority” causes running→ready only if a preemptive policy chooses another process.OS06
An OS allocates CPU time, memory, storage, devices and communication resources, and provides services to programs.OS01
Shared memory lets processes access a common region and requires synchronization for conflicting updates. Message passing exchanges data through send/receive operations.OS09

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Memory allocation is a separate function (p5).

B · incorrect — Process data exchange (p11) is not selection of the next CPU user.

C · incorrect — Device allocation (p6) does not decide CPU order.

D · correct — CPU scheduling selects ready processes (p20).

Question 4

Which one of the following is not true?

  • A. kernel is the program that constitutes the central core of the operating system
  • B. kernel is the first part of operating system to load into memory during booting
  • C. kernel is made of various modules which cannot be loaded in running operating system
  • D. kernel remains in the memory during the entire computer session

Sources

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · 2016 · Question 92

  • Computer Science model exam · Question 95

  • University of Gondar model exam · Question 95

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish command interfaces, privileged core services and the stages of booting.

Study-guide sections

OS03 · Kernel, shell, privilege and boot

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS03 · Kernel, shell, privilege and boot

Correct answer: C — The claim that kernel modules cannot load while running is false.

OS03 states that loadable modules can add drivers to a running kernel, directly contradicting option C’s absolute prohibition. It describes the kernel as the core OS component and resident through the session. Option B is read as the first core OS component, rather than the first code executed at power-on: firmware and bootloader stages precede it.

Evidence in the complete study note

The kernel implements core resource management, protection and system-call handling.OS03
Firmware/bootloader stages execute before transferring control to a kernel loaded into RAM. The kernel is the first core OS component, not the first program ever executed at power-on. Loadable modules can add drivers to a running kernel; “kernel modules cannot be loaded while running” is false in modular systems. A resident kernel remains active through the session. Utilities such as antivirus and disk tools can combine user processes with privileged components.OS03

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — The kernel implements core resource management, protection and system-call handling, so this statement is true.

B · incorrect — As the first core OS component, the kernel is loaded at boot; firmware/bootloader execution precedes that OS boundary.

C · correct — Modular kernels permit runtime module loading, directly refuting the statement that it cannot occur.

D · incorrect — A resident kernel remains active throughout the session, matching the note’s ordinary kernel model.

Question 5

One is not the goal of I/O software

  • A. Buffering
  • B. Device independence
  • C. Uniform naming
  • D. Error handling
  • E. None of the above

Source

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 66

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Separate device hardware, device-specific software and general event or transfer mechanisms.

Study-guide sections

OS34 · Device controllers, drivers and I/O services

OS35 · Buffering, caching, spooling and clocks

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS34 · Device controllers, drivers and I/O services

OS35 · Buffering, caching, spooling and clocks

Correct answer: E — None: all four are I/O-software goals or supporting functions.

OS34 identifies device independence, uniform naming and error handling as I/O services. OS35 explains buffering as holding in-transit data to absorb speed/size differences. Each listed feature therefore supports I/O software, so none is an exclusion. Calling buffering a supporting mechanism rather than a high-level objective does not make it unrelated to I/O software’s goals.

Evidence in the complete study note

A driver converts OS requests into device-specific operations. Device independence and uniform naming hide brand-specific details; error handling reports/retries supported failures.OS34
Buffering temporarily holds data in transit to absorb speed/transfer-size differences.OS35

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Buffering absorbs transfer-speed and size differences, directly supporting reliable and efficient I/O software.

B · incorrect — Device independence is an explicit I/O service that hides device-specific details from higher layers.

C · incorrect — Uniform naming is explicitly included among the I/O services described here.

D · incorrect — Reporting or retrying supported device failures is the error-handling responsibility of I/O software.

E · correct — All four listed functions support I/O software, leaving no listed function to exclude.

Question 6

Which hardware unit translates a process’s logical/virtual addresses to physical memory addresses during execution?

  • A. MMU
  • B. IRQ
  • C. DMA
  • D. Cache
  • E. Relocation Register

Adapted from

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 67

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Track when an address or library reference is resolved and the direction of translation.

Study-guide sections

OS27 · Address binding, dynamic loading and linking

OS28 · Contiguous allocation and fit policies

OS34 · Device controllers, drivers and I/O services

OS35 · Buffering, caching, spooling and clocks

OS-S04 · Communication endpoints and timed I/O events

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS27 · Address binding, dynamic loading and linking

OS28 · Contiguous allocation and fit policies

OS34 · Device controllers, drivers and I/O services

OS35 · Buffering, caching, spooling and clocks

OS-S04 · Communication endpoints and timed I/O events

Correct answer: A — MMU

The MMU performs execution-time translation from a process’s logical or virtual address to a physical RAM address. A relocation register can supply a base used in a simple translation, but it is a register rather than the complete translating unit. IRQ concerns interrupts, DMA moves data, and a cache stores reusable information. The corrected direction therefore identifies the MMU uniquely.

Evidence in the complete study note

Logical/virtual addresses are generated for a process; physical addresses select RAM. An MMU translates logical addresses to physical addresses, not the reverse.OS27
In the standard base/limit model, base is that process’s physical starting address and limit is its permitted length. Check 0≤logical_offset<limit, then physical=base+offset.OS28
Polling checks readiness repeatedly; interrupts request CPU attention; DMA transfers blocks after setup without CPU work for every byte.OS34
Caching retains copies to accelerate reuse.OS35
IRQ means interrupt request.OS-S04

Choice by choice

A · correct — The MMU is the hardware unit performing logical-to-physical translation.

B · incorrect — An IRQ signals an interrupt rather than translating process addresses.

C · incorrect — DMA transfers data between devices and memory after setup.

D · incorrect — A cache retains reusable contents; it is not the general address-translating unit.

E · incorrect — A relocation register holds a base parameter used by translation hardware.

Question 7

For holes of sizes 100, 500, 200 and 300 in that scan order and a request of size 180, which policy is better at minimizing this request’s leftover space in its selected hole? Ignore search cost and future requests.

  • A. First fit algorithm is better
  • B. Best fit algorithm is better
  • C. Worst fit algorithm is better
  • D. All of the above

Adapted from

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 68

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Apply the stated hole-selection or base-and-bound rule with its explicit convention.

Study-guide sections

OS28 · Contiguous allocation and fit policies

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS28 · Contiguous allocation and fit policies

Correct answer: B — Best fit algorithm is better

Best-fit chooses the smallest hole large enough for the request: 200, leaving 20 unused units. First-fit stops at 500 and leaves 320 units; worst-fit also chooses 500. Under the stated objective, best-fit gives the smallest remainder for this allocation. This calculation says nothing about future fragmentation or overall search speed, which require a broader workload.

Evidence in the complete study note

First-fit selects the first adequate hole; next-fit continues from the previous search position; best-fit selects the smallest adequate hole; worst-fit selects the largest.OS28

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — First-fit selects 500 in this scan order, leaving 320 rather than the minimum remainder.

B · correct — Best-fit selects 200 and leaves only 20 units.

C · incorrect — Worst-fit selects 500 and leaves 320 units.

D · incorrect — The policies do not tie: only best-fit minimizes this request’s leftover space.

Question 8

All jobs arrive together at submission, have known finite CPU bursts, perform no I/O, and no further jobs arrive. Assume one CPU, zero switching overhead, known fixed priorities (smaller value is higher; non-preemptive priority), a known positive RR quantum, and listed order for ties. For which listed algorithms can each job’s total waiting time be computed at submission? Choose the most complete answer.

  • A. Shortest job first
  • B. Shortest remaining time
  • C. Priority based
  • D. Round Robin
  • E. All of the above

Adapted from

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 69

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Construct a timeline and distinguish completion rates from individual elapsed-time measures.

Study-guide sections

OS13 · Scheduling metrics and calculation method

OS16 · SJF/SJN and shortest remaining time

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

OS18 · Round Robin, quantum and bounded turns

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS13 · Scheduling metrics and calculation method

OS16 · SJF/SJN and shortest remaining time

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

OS18 · Round Robin, quantum and bounded turns

Correct answer: E — All of the above

The closed workload and explicit policy parameters determine each scheduling timeline. SJF orders known bursts, SRTF uses remaining bursts, priority uses the supplied fixed order, and RR uses its quantum and ready queue. Completion minus arrival minus burst then gives waiting time. Each named algorithm is individually computable, so the aggregate option is the most complete answer under these assumptions.

Evidence in the complete study note

For arrival A, first start S and completion C, response=S−A and turnaround=C−A. For a single CPU burst B with no I/O, waiting=C−A−B.OS13
Non-preemptive SJF/SJN chooses the shortest next burst among currently ready jobs. SRTF reconsiders on arrivals and selects the shortest remaining work; a shorter newcomer can preempt.OS16
Priority scheduling chooses the highest-priority ready job. Check whether a smaller or larger numeric value means higher priority. State whether a high-priority arrival preempts.OS17
RR serves a ready queue cyclically for at most quantum q per turn. Unfinished jobs return to the tail; completed/blocked jobs leave.OS18

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — SJF waiting is computable, but this option omits the other computable policies.

B · incorrect — SRTF waiting is computable for the known closed workload, but this is incomplete.

C · incorrect — Fixed-priority waiting is computable with the supplied priorities and tie rule, but this is incomplete.

D · incorrect — RR waiting is computable with the known quantum and queue order, but this is incomplete.

E · correct — All four schedules and their total waiting times can be computed from the fully specified finite workload.

Question 9

One is not a problem caused by concurrency of processes

  • A. Race condition
  • B. Starvation
  • C. Deadlock
  • D. Mutual exclusion
  • E. None

Source

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 70

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify conflicting shared accesses and the safety or progress property required.

Study-guide sections

OS10 · Races, critical sections and correctness

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS10 · Races, critical sections and correctness

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

Correct answer: D — Mutual exclusion is a desired safety property.

OS10 explicitly contrasts mutual exclusion, a desired safety property, with race conditions, starvation and deadlock as possible failures. Although a badly designed locking scheme can introduce deadlock, that does not turn the mutual-exclusion requirement itself into a concurrency failure. Therefore it is the single option that is not a problem in the sense used by the list.

Evidence in the complete study note

A race occurs when results depend on uncoordinated interleaving of conflicting accesses. A critical section is code accessing shared state that needs protection, not the resource itself. A correct exclusion solution requires mutual exclusion, progress and bounded waiting under stated assumptions. Mutual exclusion is a desired safety property; races, starvation and deadlock are possible failures.OS10
A set is deadlocked when each member waits for an event/resource that only another blocked member can provide. Mere waiting for another holder is normal blocking; indefinite unfair delay can be starvation without a cycle of dependencies.OS22

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A race is a concurrency failure caused by uncoordinated interleaving of conflicting accesses.

B · incorrect — Starvation is a possible indefinite-delay failure of concurrent scheduling or resource competition.

C · incorrect — Deadlock is a possible failure in which blocked participants cannot supply one another’s required events.

D · correct — Mutual exclusion is a correctness property protecting shared state, rather than a failure to be avoided.

E · incorrect — Mutual exclusion provides a clear non-failure option, so none of the choices is not appropriate.

Question 10

Which methods can participate in recovery after deadlock has been detected? Assume checkpoints and safe resource reclamation are available where needed. Select all that apply.

  • A. Kill the process
  • B. Rollback
  • C. Non-preempt the resource
  • D. Lock the process

Adapted from

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 71

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Separate handling before allocation from actions taken after detection, and consider the cost of checking.

Study-guide sections

OS26 · Detection, recovery and intentional ignoring

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS26 · Detection, recovery and intentional ignoring

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

Correct answers (select all): A, B — Kill the process; Rollback

Terminating a selected process can release resources and break the dependency cycle. Recovery may instead reclaim safely preemptable resources and restore affected work to a checkpoint, so rollback can also participate. Merely preserving non-preemption or locking a process does not remove the deadlocked dependencies. With checkpoint and reclamation support stated, both termination and rollback are valid recovery tools.

Evidence in the complete study note

Detection asks whether the current state is deadlocked. Recovery can terminate selected processes or reclaim safely preemptable resources and roll affected work back to a checkpoint. Termination and rollback are both possible recovery tools; choose according to the resource and process conditions.OS26
No resource preemption means release must be voluntary.OS22

Choice by choice

A · correct — Killing a selected victim can release resources and break the deadlock.

B · correct — Rollback can restore affected work after safe resource reclamation.

C · incorrect — Non-preemption preserves a deadlock-enabling restriction rather than recovering the processes.

D · incorrect — Locking a blocked process does not release resources or restore progress.

Question 11

One deadlock prevention method says, "if a process must wait for a needed resource, it drops all of its previously held resources and tries to acquire all resources gain." These attacks

  • A. Circular wait condition
  • B. No preemption conditions
  • C. Mutual exclusion condition
  • D. Hold and wait condition
  • E. None of the above

Source

  • Addis Ababa University mock exam · Question 72

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify the resource-request restriction and which necessary condition it removes.

Study-guide sections

OS24 · Prevention: identify the condition removed

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS24 · Prevention: identify the condition removed

Correct answer: D — The policy removes hold-and-wait.

Under this policy, a process releases all previously held resources when it must wait. Releasing all resources before retrying removes simultaneous holding while waiting. The stem describes voluntary dropping, not forced withdrawal by another agent, so hold-and-wait is the directly attacked condition. It supplies no global ordering or resource-sharing rule.

Evidence in the complete study note

Prevention constrains how requests are made. Share a resource where safe to remove mutual exclusion; request all needed resources before holding any to remove hold-and-wait; permit safe resource withdrawal/restart to attack no-preemption; impose a global acquisition order to remove circular wait. Not every physical resource is shareable or safely preemptable.OS24
“If waiting, release all held resources and try again” removes simultaneous holding while waiting.OS24

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — The policy does not impose a global acquisition order, which is the direct circular-wait prevention method.

B · incorrect — The stem specifies the process dropping resources, not a general rule permitting forced resource preemption.

C · incorrect — Resources may remain nonshareable; releasing holdings does not remove mutual-exclusion requirements.

D · correct — A process no longer retains allocated resources while waiting to acquire the rest.

E · incorrect — Hold-and-wait is explicitly removed by the release-all-before-waiting rule, so an identified condition fits.

Question 12

Which statements are false? Interpret aging under a priority scheme and workload where increasing waiting jobs’ priority prevents indefinite postponement. Select all that apply.

  • A. File system standards of various OS is the same.
  • B. SJF may cause starvation.
  • C. Aging is the solution of the starvation problem in the case of priority scheduling.
  • D. Critical section is a resource that cannot be accessed by multiple processes concurrently.

Adapted from

  • Mock exam 4 · Question 31

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify conflicting shared accesses and the safety or progress property required.

Study-guide sections

OS10 · Races, critical sections and correctness

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

OS36 · Files, descriptors and ordinary operations

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS10 · Races, critical sections and correctness

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

OS36 · Files, descriptors and ordinary operations

Correct answers (select all): A, D — File system standards of various OS is the same.; Critical section is a resource that cannot be accessed by multiple processes concurrently.

Filesystems are not governed by one identical format across all operating systems; the notes distinguish FAT32, NTFS and ext4. A critical section is code that accesses shared state, not the resource itself. SJF can starve a long job when short work continues arriving. Aging addresses priority starvation under the stated policy assumptions, so those latter two statements are true.

Evidence in the complete study note

A critical section is code accessing shared state that needs protection, not the resource itself.OS10
Fixed-priority scheduling can starve low-priority jobs under continued high-priority load. SJF can also starve long jobs; starvation is not exclusive to fixed-priority scheduling.OS17
Aging increases waiting jobs’ effective priority according to a rule, reducing indefinite postponement when the priority scheme and workload permit.OS17
Sockets also support ordinary read/write, alongside socket/connect/send operations, so read is not uniquely “not a network call.” FAT32, NTFS and ext4 are filesystem formats; HTTP is an application communication protocol.OS36

Choice by choice

A · correct — The universal sameness claim is false; different filesystem formats exist.

B · incorrect — SJF may starve long work under repeated short arrivals, so this statement is true.

C · incorrect — Aging raises effective priority and addresses starvation under the specified conditions, so this statement is true.

D · correct — A critical section is protected code rather than the shared resource, making this statement false.

Question 13

One of the following system calls is used to create a new process.

  • A. Fork()
  • B. exit()
  • C. abort()
  • D. exec()

Source

  • Mock exam 4 · Question 32

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Separate creation, image replacement and termination; classify termination by its cause.

Study-guide sections

OS07 · Creation, fork/exec and termination

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS07 · Creation, fork/exec and termination

Correct answer: A — fork().

OS07 states that fork() creates a child in POSIX-style systems, returning zero in the child and its PID in the parent on success. exec replaces the current process image and does not itself create another process. exit and abort terminate execution normally and abnormally respectively. Creation therefore corresponds to fork among the literal listed calls.

Evidence in the complete study note

In POSIX-style systems fork() creates a child: zero is returned in the child and the child PID in the parent on success. The two have distinct address spaces, although resources may be shared through explicit mechanisms. exec replaces the current process image; it does not itself create another process.OS07
Normal completion and a program-detected error exit are voluntary; an unrecoverable fault or being killed is involuntary. exit() terminates normally through the runtime; abort() requests abnormal termination.OS07

Choice by choice

A · correct — fork creates a child process rather than replacing or terminating the existing process image.

B · incorrect — exit terminates normally through the runtime rather than creating a new process.

C · incorrect — abort requests abnormal termination rather than allocating a new child execution instance.

D · incorrect — exec replaces the current process image; it does not itself create another process.

Question 14

Which of the following process scheduling algorithms select the next process to be executed based on each process burst time?

  • A. FCFS
  • B. Shortest Job First
  • C. Round robin
  • D. Priority scheduling algorithm

Source

  • Mock exam 4 · Question 34

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare the ready jobs using the stated burst or remaining-time rule.

Study-guide sections

OS16 · SJF/SJN and shortest remaining time

OS15 · FCFS and the convoy effect

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

OS18 · Round Robin, quantum and bounded turns

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS16 · SJF/SJN and shortest remaining time

OS15 · FCFS and the convoy effect

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

OS18 · Round Robin, quantum and bounded turns

Correct answer: B — Shortest Job First

SJF requires knowing how long a process will take (PDF p23); that is the burst-length selection described. Priority may use time as one possible priority input (p24), but its general rule is highest assigned priority.

Evidence in the complete study note

Non-preemptive SJF/SJN chooses the shortest next burst among currently ready jobs.OS16
FCFS runs ready bursts in arrival order and ordinarily does not preempt.OS15
Priority scheduling chooses the highest-priority ready job.OS17
RR serves a ready queue cyclically for at most quantum q per turn.OS18

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — FCFS orders arrival (p23), not burst length.

B · correct — SJF chooses short work based on execution duration (p23).

C · incorrect — RR assigns a fixed quantum (p25).

D · incorrect — Priority selects assigned priority; time requirements are only one possible input (p24).

Question 15

__________ is a software solution of a critical section problem if the number of instances and processes are more than two.

  • A. Semaphore
  • B. Compare and swap
  • C. Peterson
  • D. Test set

Source

  • Mock exam 4 · Question 35

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Track available permits and blocked operations using the stated semaphore model.

Study-guide sections

OS11 · Semaphores and resource-count traces

OS12 · Turn variables and atomic primitives

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS11 · Semaphores and resource-count traces

OS12 · Turn variables and atomic primitives

Correct answer: A — Semaphore.

OS12 explicitly says a semaphore can coordinate more than two processes, while classic Peterson’s algorithm is for two under its memory-model assumptions. OS11 explains counting semaphores as tracking several permits, fitting multiple resource instances. Compare-and-swap and test-and-set are atomic primitives rather than the named software coordination abstraction requested by the stem.

Evidence in the complete study note

A semaphore provides atomic wait/P/down and signal/V/up operations. Under the nonnegative-token model, wait consumes one available token or blocks when none is available; signal supplies a token and may wake a waiter. Implementations using negative internal counts encode waiters differently, so state the model. A binary semaphore represents one permit; a counting semaphore tracks several. A mutex normally has ownership rules that a general semaphore lacks.OS11
Test-and-set and compare-and-swap combine checking/updating atomically; ordinary check-then-write does not. Peterson’s classic algorithm uses flags plus turn for two processes under its memory-model assumptions; a semaphore can coordinate more than two.OS12

Choice by choice

A · correct — Counting semaphores track multiple permits and coordinate more than two participating processes.

B · incorrect — Compare-and-swap is an atomic checking/updating primitive, rather than the requested software semaphore abstraction.

C · incorrect — Classic Peterson’s algorithm solves the two-process case, not the stated more-than-two configuration.

D · incorrect — Test-and-set is an atomic primitive; the software multiple-permit coordination mechanism here is a semaphore.

Question 16

A process can be terminated due to:

  • A. killed by another process
  • B. normal exit
  • C. all of the mentioned
  • D. fatal error

Sources

  • Computer Science model exam · Question 90

  • University of Gondar model exam · Question 90

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Separate creation, image replacement and termination; classify termination by its cause.

Study-guide sections

OS07 · Creation, fork/exec and termination

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS07 · Creation, fork/exec and termination

Correct answer: C — All listed causes can terminate a process.

OS07 distinguishes voluntary normal completion from involuntary faults or being killed. Each named cause is therefore a genuine termination cause, although the causes differ in who initiates them and whether completion is normal. The aggregate choice includes all three. Selecting only one named cause would omit other valid termination paths supported by the complete note.

Evidence in the complete study note

Normal completion and a program-detected error exit are voluntary; an unrecoverable fault or being killed is involuntary. exit() terminates normally through the runtime; abort() requests abnormal termination.OS07

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Being killed can terminate a process, but this alone omits normal completion and fatal faults.

B · incorrect — Normal exit is valid, but it is only one of the three supported termination causes.

C · correct — Being killed, normal exit and fatal error all provide legitimate process-termination causes.

D · incorrect — A fatal error can terminate execution, but the other two named causes are also valid.

Question 17

Which named interface directly requests protected operating-system services across the user/kernel boundary, rather than merely providing application-facing wrappers?

  • A. library
  • B. system calls
  • C. assembly instructions
  • D. API

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam · Question 91

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 4

  • University of Gondar model exam · Question 91

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify the abstraction layer and separate a protected service request from an application task.

Study-guide sections

OS04 · APIs, system calls and virtualization

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS04 · APIs, system calls and virtualization

Correct answer: B — system calls

A system call requests a protected kernel service, such as process creation or file access. An application may call an API implemented by a library, but that wrapper is distinct from the direct user/kernel service boundary. Assembly can encode the machine instructions used to enter that boundary without being its general interface name. The requested abstraction is therefore system calls.

Evidence in the complete study note

A system call requests a protected kernel service, such as creating a process, reading a file or opening a socket. A user-facing API/library may wrap that request; not every library function enters the kernel. Distinguish the application API from the direct user/kernel boundary when describing access to OS services.OS04

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A library can wrap system calls but is not itself the direct kernel-service boundary.

B · correct — System calls directly request protected kernel services.

C · incorrect — Assembly instructions are a programming representation, not the named service-interface abstraction.

D · incorrect — An API is the application-facing interface and may wrap the direct system-call request.

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

OS34 · Device controllers, drivers and I/O services

Question 19

Which of the following condition is required for deadlock to be possible?

  • A. mutual exclusion
  • B. all of the mentioned
  • C. a process may hold allocated resources while awaiting assignment of other resources
  • D. no resource can be forcibly removed from a process holding it

Sources

  • Computer Science model exam · Question 93

  • University of Gondar model exam · Question 93

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace the resource dependencies and distinguish necessary conditions from evidence of an actual deadlock.

Study-guide sections

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

Correct answer: B — All named conditions are necessary conditions.

OS22 lists mutual exclusion, hold-and-wait, no resource preemption and circular wait as necessary in the standard reusable-resource model. The three substantive choices name the first three, so all of the mentioned is the comprehensive option. Circular wait is also required despite being omitted from the list. Having these policies does not prove a particular current allocation is already deadlocked.

Evidence in the complete study note

Mutual exclusion allows at most one holder of a nonshareable resource. Hold-and-wait means retaining a resource while requesting another. No resource preemption means release must be voluntary. Circular wait forms a closed chain of such waits.OS22
All four are necessary in the standard reusable-resource model.OS22

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Mutual exclusion is necessary, but selecting it alone omits the other two necessary conditions offered.

B · correct — Each substantive option names a standard necessary deadlock condition; the aggregate includes all offered conditions.

C · incorrect — Retaining resources while requesting more is hold-and-wait, one valid condition among the three offered.

D · incorrect — No forced removal is no-preemption, which is necessary but does not exclude the other valid conditions.

Jobs execute first come, first served on PDF p23. The first CPU requester is consequently the first served in the FCFS queue.

Apply the same study-note rule to this alternative source set; the choice contrasts below show the derivation.

FCFS runs ready bursts in arrival order and ordinarily does not preempt.OS15

Non-preemptive SJF/SJN chooses the shortest next burst among currently ready jobs.OS16

Priority scheduling chooses the highest-priority ready job.OS17

A · incorrect — Priority scheduling chooses assigned rank, not request order (PDF p24).

B · incorrect — Shortest-job scheduling chooses duration, not request order (PDF p23).

C · correct — FCFS serves jobs first come, first served (PDF p23). The variant moves the correct text from current A to variant C.

Question 21

Which directory implementation is used in most Operating System?

  • A. Single level directory structure
  • B. Two level directory structure
  • C. Tree directory structure
  • D. Acyclic directory structure

Follow namespace structure and distinguish names, underlying files and mounted filesystems.

Study-guide sections

OS37 · Directories, mounting and links

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS37 · Directories, mounting and links

Correct answer: C — Tree directory structure, in the usual general-purpose OS sense.

OS37 states that modern general-purpose systems commonly present hierarchical namespaces and defines a tree as allowing nested directories and absolute/relative paths. That directly supports the conventional tree-directory choice. This is a general-purpose namespace convention, not a measured claim about every OS ever built; links can add graph structure without replacing the hierarchy as the basic user-visible organization.

Evidence in the complete study note

A single-level directory requires unique names in one namespace. Two-level directories separate users. A tree allows nested directories and absolute/relative paths; links can create shared graphs. Modern general-purpose systems commonly present hierarchical namespaces, but a feature name alone does not prove that every system excludes simpler arrangements.OS37

Choice by choice

B · incorrect — Separating user directories alone is less general than the nested hierarchy specified for modern general-purpose systems.

C · correct — Nested directories and paths form the tree/hierarchical organization the note identifies as commonly presented.

D · incorrect — Links can add graph sharing, but acyclic sharing is not the basic common directory organization identified.

Question 22

In the basic virtual-memory models, which technique divides the address space into fixed-size units mapped to equal-size physical frames?

  • A. Paging
  • B. Segmentation
  • C. A&B
  • D. All

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 37

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Split the address into page and offset, then apply the mapping and access checks.

Study-guide sections

OS30 · Paging, page tables and TLB

OS31 · Segmentation and contrasts with paging

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS30 · Paging, page tables and TLB

OS31 · Segmentation and contrasts with paging

Correct answer: A — Paging

Paging divides addresses into fixed-size pages and maps them to physical frames of the same size. Segmentation instead uses variable-length logical units such as code and stack. Thus the fixed-size condition selects paging alone. Both combined alternatives include segmentation and therefore fail that condition; fixed partitions elsewhere in an operating system are outside the page-versus-segment comparison.

Evidence in the complete study note

Pages and physical frames have equal fixed size P.OS30
Segmentation uses logical variable-length units such as code, stack and arrays.OS31

Choice by choice

A · correct — Paging uses fixed-size pages and corresponding equal-size frames.

B · incorrect — Segments are variable-length logical units, so segmentation does not satisfy the fixed-unit condition.

C · incorrect — Including segmentation makes this combined choice fail the fixed-size requirement.

D · incorrect — All includes the variable-size segmentation alternative and is therefore incorrect.

Question 23

When does a page fault occur?

  • A. The page is present in memory.
  • B. Deadlock occurs.
  • C. The page does not present in memory.
  • D. Buffering occurs.

Sources

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 72

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 90

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Separate a permitted nonresident access from an illegal access and trace the required handling steps.

Study-guide sections

OS32 · Virtual memory and page-fault handling

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS32 · Virtual memory and page-fault handling

Correct answer: C — The page does not present in memory.

A demand-paging access to a nonresident page causes the trap (PDF p38). PDF p39 distinguishes a valid but absent page, which is brought in, from an invalid address, which terminates the process. C presupposes an actual reference to that page.

Evidence in the complete study note

Virtual memory provides translated, protected address spaces and allows nonresident portions of a program to be backed by storage. It does not add physical RAM or remove all capacity constraints. Demand paging brings a page in when accessed. On a nonresident access: trap, validate mapping/permissions, find a frame or choose a victim, save a dirty victim if needed, obtain contents, update mapping and restart.OS32
Not every fault reads a disk: some allocate zero-filled pages or establish shared mappings. An illegal access is rejected, not automatically loaded.OS32

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Resident page access proceeds normally (p38).

B · incorrect — Deadlock is not the page-residency test on p38.

C · correct — An accessed valid page absent from RAM causes demand paging (pp38–39).

D · incorrect — Buffering is not the fault-triggering residency condition.

Question 24

Which of the following is a condition that causes deadlock?

  • A. Mutual exclusion
  • B. Hold and wait
  • C. Circular wait
  • D. No preemption
  • E. All of these

Source

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 73

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace the resource dependencies and distinguish necessary conditions from evidence of an actual deadlock.

Study-guide sections

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

Correct answer: E — All four are standard necessary deadlock conditions.

OS22 lists mutual exclusion, hold-and-wait, no-preemption and circular wait as the four necessary conditions in the standard reusable-resource model. Each substantive choice therefore names a valid deadlock condition, making all of these the comprehensive answer. The wording causes must not be read as saying one condition alone is sufficient; actual allocation/request dependencies still require inspection.

Evidence in the complete study note

Mutual exclusion allows at most one holder of a nonshareable resource. Hold-and-wait means retaining a resource while requesting another. No resource preemption means release must be voluntary. Circular wait forms a closed chain of such waits.OS22
All four are necessary in the standard reusable-resource model. Their availability as general system policies does not prove a deadlock exists at this instant; inspect actual allocations/requests.OS22

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Mutual exclusion is one necessary condition, but the other three named conditions also belong to the standard set.

B · incorrect — Hold-and-wait is necessary, but selecting it alone leaves out the other valid conditions.

C · incorrect — Circular wait is part of the condition set, but it is not the sole valid listed condition.

D · incorrect — No-preemption is necessary in this model, alongside the other three substantive choices.

E · correct — All four substantive choices are standard necessary conditions; together their names give the complete condition set.

Question 25

What is an operating system?

  • B. Collection of programs that manages hardware resources

OS01 · Objectives, resource management and evolution

OS04 · APIs, system calls and virtualization

The OS manages hardware and mediates users/program execution (PDF p5), and provides services to programs (p10). Thus each of A–C is a valid aspect of the definition and D collects them.

An OS allocates CPU time, memory, storage, devices and communication resources, and provides services to programs. Question 26

What is the ready state of a process?

  • A. When process is unable to run until some task has been completed
  • B. When process is scheduled to run after some execution
  • C. When process is using the CPU
  • D. None of the mentioned

Source

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 76

Decide whether the process is eligible, running or waiting, then identify the responsible queue or scheduling role.

Study-guide sections

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Correct answer: B — When process is scheduled to run after some execution

Ready means waiting to be assigned a processor (PDF p16). B is the nearest paraphrase, provided scheduled to run means eligible and waiting, not already executing.

Evidence in the complete study note

New processes are created/admitted; ready means eligible for CPU but not currently running. Running uses a CPU. Waiting/blocked means unable to proceed until an event; terminated means execution has ended. Running→ready follows time-slice expiry or another preemption. Running→waiting follows blocking I/O. I/O completion normally gives waiting→ready, not a guaranteed immediate running state.OS06

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Waiting for an event is the Waiting state.

B · correct — Eligible to receive CPU next matches Ready under the stated reading.

C · incorrect — Using the CPU defines Running.

D · incorrect — Ready is represented by B under the stated interpretation.

Question 27

In the standard reusable-resource model, a set of processes is deadlocked when ____________.

  • A. Each process is terminated
  • B. All processes are trying to kill each other
  • C. Each process is blocked waiting for an event or resource that only another blocked member of the set can provide
  • D. None of the mentioned

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 77

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace the resource dependencies and distinguish necessary conditions from evidence of an actual deadlock.

Study-guide sections

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

OS07 · Creation, fork/exec and termination

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

OS07 · Creation, fork/exec and termination

Correct answer: C — Each process is blocked waiting for an event or resource that only another blocked member of the set can provide

Deadlock requires blocked participants whose needed event or resource can be supplied only by another blocked participant in the same set. The closed dependency prevents any member from progressing without intervention. Terminated processes are no longer waiting, and attempts to kill one another do not establish a resource dependency. The revised blocking option states the necessary relationship rather than merely predicting permanent delay.

Evidence in the complete study note

A set is deadlocked when each member waits for an event/resource that only another blocked member can provide. Mere waiting for another holder is normal blocking; indefinite unfair delay can be starvation without a cycle of dependencies.OS22
Normal completion and a program-detected error exit are voluntary; an unrecoverable fault or being killed is involuntary. exit() terminates normally through the runtime; abort() requests abnormal termination.OS07

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Termination ends execution and does not define a set of blocked dependent processes.

B · incorrect — Attempts to kill other processes do not establish deadlocked resource waits.

C · correct — This states the closed dependency among blocked members that defines deadlock.

D · incorrect — The revised C explicitly gives the deadlock definition, so none is false.

Question 29

In which of the storage placement strategies a program is placed in the largest available hole in the main memory?

  • A. Best fit
  • B. First fit
  • C. Worst fit
  • D. Buddy

Source

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 79

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Apply the stated hole-selection or base-and-bound rule with its explicit convention.

Study-guide sections

OS28 · Contiguous allocation and fit policies

OS-S03 · Memory allocation and page-policy decisions

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS28 · Contiguous allocation and fit policies

OS-S03 · Memory allocation and page-policy decisions

Correct answer: C — Worst-fit.

OS28 defines worst-fit as selecting the largest hole, directly matching the stem. Best-fit chooses the smallest adequate hole, and first-fit stops at the first adequate one rather than comparing all sizes. The buddy label does not state this largest-hole rule in the provided note. No numeric trace is necessary because the selection criterion itself is explicit.

Evidence in the complete study note

First-fit selects the first adequate hole; next-fit continues from the previous search position; best-fit selects the smallest adequate hole; worst-fit selects the largest.OS28
A buddy allocator manages power-of-two block sizes. It takes a smallest sufficient block, splitting larger blocks when necessary, and can merge a freed block with its free buddy. This is not the worst-fit rule of selecting the largest arbitrary hole.OS-S03

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Best-fit chooses the smallest adequate hole, which is the opposite size-selection criterion from the stem.

B · incorrect — First-fit selects the first adequate hole in search order, not necessarily the largest available hole.

C · correct — Worst-fit is explicitly the policy that selects the largest available adequate hole.

D · incorrect — The supplied largest-hole criterion is defined as worst-fit; the buddy label is not that rule.

Question 33

In asystem, resources cannot be forcibly taken from processes; they must be released voluntarily. Which deadlock condition does this represent?

  • A. No Preemption
  • B. Hold and Wait
  • C. . Mutual Exclusion
  • D. Circular Wait

Source

  • Computer Science Exit Exam · 2018 · Question 57

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace the resource dependencies and distinguish necessary conditions from evidence of an actual deadlock.

Study-guide sections

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

Correct answer: A — No preemption.

OS22 directly defines no resource preemption as requiring voluntary release. The stem repeats that resources cannot be forcibly taken, so the match is exact. Hold-and-wait concerns retaining one resource while requesting another, mutual exclusion concerns exclusive possession, and circular wait concerns a closed dependency chain. None of those separately states the prohibition on forced withdrawal.

Evidence in the complete study note

Mutual exclusion allows at most one holder of a nonshareable resource. Hold-and-wait means retaining a resource while requesting another. No resource preemption means release must be voluntary. Circular wait forms a closed chain of such waits.OS22

Choice by choice

A · correct — Prohibiting forced withdrawal and requiring voluntary release is exactly the no-preemption resource condition.

B · incorrect — Hold-and-wait concerns retaining a resource while requesting another, not whether forced removal is permitted.

C · incorrect — Mutual exclusion limits concurrent holders; it does not itself require voluntary release.

D · incorrect — Circular wait describes a closed chain of dependencies rather than the resource-withdrawal rule.

Question 34

How does an operating system improve efficiency in a multi-user environment?

  • A. By allowing only one user to access the system at a time
  • B. By disabling all background services
  • C. . By using time-sharing and process scheduling to allocate CPU time fairly among users and processes
  • D. By requiring manual intervention for each task

Source

  • Computer Science Exit Exam · 2018 · Question 97

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare interaction, CPU sharing and deadline requirements under the stated system model.

Study-guide sections

OS02 · Batch, multiprogramming, time-sharing and real time

OS20 · Fair-share, proportional shares and guarantees

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS02 · Batch, multiprogramming, time-sharing and real time

OS20 · Fair-share, proportional shares and guarantees

Correct answer: C — . By using time-sharing and process scheduling to allocate CPU time fairly among users and processes

Time-sharing switches CPU among users and gives each a small portion of time (PDF p7), reducing idle time and giving quick response. C captures that scheduling-based allocation.

Evidence in the complete study note

Batch jobs run with little interaction after submission. Multiprogramming keeps several jobs available so another can use the CPU while one waits; it does not require fixed time slices. Time-sharing uses rapid scheduling/preemption to support interactive users. A single CPU interleaves execution; multiple CPUs can execute work truly in parallel. Shared-memory multiprocessors do not require each CPU to have private main memory.OS02
Fair-share scheduling allocates according to stated weights, potentially per user/group rather than per process.OS20

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — One user at a time contradicts the simultaneous time-sharing service on p7.

B · incorrect — Disabling background services is not the mechanism described.

C · correct — CPU scheduling and time-sharing are explicitly the mechanism on p7.

D · incorrect — Manual intervention per task is not the CPU-switching mechanism.

Question 35

Which exits are involuntary? Here “Terminate exit” means an externally imposed kill, “Fatal exit” means an unrecoverable execution fault, and “Error exit” means the program chooses to exit after detecting bad input. Select all that apply.

  • A. Normal exit
  • B. Terminate exit
  • C. Fatal exit
  • D. Error exit

Adapted from

  • National Exit Exam practice questions · Question 8

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice collection · Question 217

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Separate creation, image replacement and termination; classify termination by its cause.

Study-guide sections

OS07 · Creation, fork/exec and termination

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS07 · Creation, fork/exec and termination

Correct answers (select all): B, C — Terminate exit; Fatal exit

An externally imposed kill and an unrecoverable execution fault stop a process involuntarily. Normal completion is chosen by the program, and detecting an input error then exiting is also a voluntary program decision. The meanings separate causes of termination instead of treating every use of the word terminate as one category. Thus the external-kill and fatal-fault choices are selected.

Evidence in the complete study note

Normal completion and a program-detected error exit are voluntary; an unrecoverable fault or being killed is involuntary. exit() terminates normally through the runtime; abort() requests abnormal termination.OS07

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Normal completion is a voluntary exit.

B · correct — The defined external kill imposes termination and is involuntary.

C · correct — An unrecoverable execution fault imposes termination and is involuntary.

D · incorrect — The program-detected error exit is a voluntary decision under the stated meaning.

Question 36

How do we define the term Thread?

  • A. Device that controls input
  • B. Variable that controls movement
  • C. Controlled execution of applications
  • D. None of the above

Source

  • Web Programming practice questions · Question 18

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare per-thread execution context with shared process resources and the stated thread mapping.

Study-guide sections

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

Correct answer: D — None of the above

A thread is a separate flow of execution through process code with its own PC, registers and stack (PDF p27). None of A–C states that definition; controlled execution of applications is too broad to identify a thread.

Evidence in the complete study note

A thread has its own PC, registers and stack. Threads in a process share code, address space and usually open-file resources. A process can contain many threads; a thread belongs to one process at a time. Threads are not a group of processes and need not execute sequentially. Independent processes can exist; cooperating ones communicate/share data.OS08

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A device is not the defined flow of execution.

B · incorrect — A variable is not a flow of control with a stack and PC.

C · incorrect — Generic controlled application execution does not distinguish thread from process or scheduler.

D · correct — None of A–C gives the thread definition on p27.

Question 37

Where are the list of processes that are prepared to be executed and waiting placed?

  • A. Job queue
  • B. Ready queue
  • C. Execution queue
  • D. Process queue

Study-guide references

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Correct answer: B — Ready queue

PDF p19 says processes wait in the ready queue for CPU allocation. That is exactly prepared to execute but awaiting a processor.

Evidence in the complete study note

The ready queue contains eligible work; device queues hold blocked requests. The broad textbook job queue contains processes in the system, while an admission queue more narrowly holds jobs awaiting memory/admission. Question 38

CPU scheduling is the basis of ___________

  • A. Multiprogramming operating systems
  • B. Larger memory-sized systems
  • C. Multiprocessor systems
  • D. None of the mentioned

Study-guide references

OS02 · Batch, multiprogramming, time-sharing and real time

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Correct answer: A — Multiprogramming operating systems

Process scheduling is explicitly essential to a multiprogramming OS (PDF p18), enabling loaded processes to time-share one CPU; multiple processors or unusually large RAM are not required.

Evidence in the complete study note

Multiprogramming keeps several jobs available so another can use the CPU while one waits; it does not require fixed time slices. Time-sharing uses rapid scheduling/preemption to support interactive users. A single CPU interleaves execution; multiple CPUs can execute work truly in parallel.OS02
Long-term scheduling admits jobs; medium-term scheduling suspends/swaps them; short-term scheduling selects ready work.OS06

Question 39

An OS designer must choose between checking for deadlock after every resource request and checking periodically. No detection cost, request rate or acceptable delay is specified. Which conclusion about a universally best checking schedule is justified?

  • A. Both checking on every request and checking periodically are always equally optimal
  • B. Checking at fixed time intervals is always optimal
  • C. Checking on every resource request is always optimal
  • D. Neither schedule is universally best; the choice trades detection overhead against delay

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 72

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Separate handling before allocation from actions taken after detection, and consider the cost of checking.

Study-guide sections

OS26 · Detection, recovery and intentional ignoring

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS26 · Detection, recovery and intentional ignoring

Correct answer: D — Neither schedule is universally best; the choice trades detection overhead against delay

Checking after every request can discover a problem promptly but incurs repeated detection overhead. Periodic checking reduces check frequency while allowing a deadlock to persist until the next check. Which tradeoff is suitable depends on workload and acceptable detection delay. Without those criteria neither schedule is universally optimal, nor is there a basis for claiming they are always equally good.

Evidence in the complete study note

Checking on every request versus periodically trades overhead against detection delay; no universal best interval exists.OS26

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — The two policies need not have equal overhead or detection delay.

B · incorrect — Fixed intervals may suit some workloads, but no universal optimum follows.

C · incorrect — Per-request checks can impose excessive overhead despite earlier detection.

D · correct — This states the overhead-versus-delay tradeoff without asserting an unsupported universal optimum.

Question 40

Which listed operation is directly an OS/tool task? Here “bad-block recovery” specifically means autonomous drive-internal sector remapping, and “booting from disk” means the initial power-on stage before the kernel has been loaded.

  • A. bad-block recovery
  • B. booting from disk
  • C. disk initialization
  • D. all of the mentioned

Adapted from

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 86

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify whether data is in transit, retained for reuse or queued for a device.

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS35 · Buffering, caching, spooling and clocks

OS03 · Kernel, shell, privilege and boot

Correct answer: C — disk initialization

Disk initialization and filesystem creation are identified as OS/tool work. Firmware starts the boot sequence before the kernel takes control, while a drive may use its own firmware to remap defective sectors. The question asks about that direct responsibility boundary, rather than broad coordination of every disk-related activity. Consequently disk initialization is the only matching choice, and the aggregate is too broad.

Evidence in the complete study note

Disk initialization/filesystem creation is OS/tool work, firmware begins boot, and drive firmware may remap bad sectors.OS35
Firmware/bootloader stages execute before transferring control to a kernel loaded into RAM.OS03

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — The specified bad-sector remapping is drive-firmware work, not the requested OS/tool operation.

B · incorrect — The stage beginning boot is firmware work before kernel control.

C · correct — Disk initialization/filesystem preparation is OS/tool work.

D · incorrect — The other operations include firmware responsibilities, so all is too broad.

Question 41

Which method is used to prevent threads or processes from accessing a single resource?

  • A. PCB
  • B. Semaphore
  • C. Job Scheduler
  • D. Non-Contiguous Memory Allocation

Source

  • Computer Science model exam 2 · Question 95

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Track available permits and blocked operations using the stated semaphore model.

Study-guide sections

OS11 · Semaphores and resource-count traces

OS05 · Programs, processes and PCB contents

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS11 · Semaphores and resource-count traces

OS05 · Programs, processes and PCB contents

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Correct answer: B — A semaphore can enforce one-at-a-time access.

OS11 describes a binary semaphore as one permit and gives wait(m); critical work; signal(m) with m initialized to 1. This prevents competing threads/processes from entering the protected resource operation simultaneously; it does not mean nobody can ever use the resource. A PCB stores process state, a scheduler selects jobs, and allocation layout does not enforce this resource-access rule.

Evidence in the complete study note

A semaphore provides atomic wait/P/down and signal/V/up operations. Under the nonnegative-token model, wait consumes one available token or blocks when none is available; signal supplies a token and may wake a waiter. Implementations using negative internal counts encode waiters differently, so state the model. A binary semaphore represents one permit; a counting semaphore tracks several. A mutex normally has ownership rules that a general semaphore lacks.OS11
Initialize a mutex semaphore to 1. Each thread does wait(m); critical work; signal(m). For a buffer of N slots use empty=N, full=0 and mutex=1: producer waits empty then mutex, inserts, unlocks then signals full. Waiting for empty while already holding mutex can prevent the consumer from freeing a slot.OS11
A saved PC identifies where execution resumes.OS05
Long-term scheduling admits jobs; medium-term scheduling suspends/swaps them; short-term scheduling selects ready work.OS06

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A PCB records execution and resource state rather than supplying atomic mutual-exclusion operations.

B · correct — A one-permit semaphore with atomic wait/signal can serialize entry to the protected resource operation.

C · incorrect — A job scheduler selects admitted or ready work; it does not by itself protect shared resource accesses.

D · incorrect — Noncontiguous memory placement changes allocation layout rather than synchronizing access to one shared resource.

OS40 · Linux, Android and product-history limits

Correct answer: D — Microsoft Office is an application suite.

The kernel-versus-distribution distinction for Linux does not turn it into an office application or invalidate the conventional OS-family contrast.

Windows and macOS are both proprietary products, although macOS contains open-source components, so “which is not open source?” offering both is ambiguous. Microsoft Office is an application suite.OS40

D · correct — Microsoft Office is explicitly described as an application suite, not an operating system.

Question 43

What is the main function of an operating system?

  • A. To manage hardware resources
  • B. To manage software applications
  • C. To manage user accounts
  • D. To manage network connections

Source

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 2

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Separate broad resource-management goals from individual services or applications.

Study-guide sections

OS01 · Objectives, resource management and evolution

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS01 · Objectives, resource management and evolution

Correct answer: A — To manage hardware resources

Hardware resource management is the general technical definition on PDF p5. Applications, accounts and connections are narrower services and cannot replace that umbrella definition.

Evidence in the complete study note

An OS allocates CPU time, memory, storage, devices and communication resources, and provides services to programs. Convenience means making tasks easy through consistent interfaces, files and device abstractions. Efficiency means using resources effectively; it is not synonymous with the fastest response for every process. Application development tools use OS services but application development itself is not a core resource-management function.OS01

Choice by choice

A · correct — Matches the hardware-management definition (p5).

B · incorrect — Application execution is a service within that broader role.

C · incorrect — Accounts are one aspect of network/user administration (p8).

D · incorrect — Network connection management is narrower than OS-wide hardware management (p8).

Question 44

Which of the following is not a memory management technique used in operating systems?

  • A. Paging
  • B. Segmentation
  • C. Swapping
  • D. Virtualization

Source

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 3

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify the abstraction layer and separate a protected service request from an application task.

Study-guide sections

OS04 · APIs, system calls and virtualization

OS27 · Address binding, dynamic loading and linking

OS30 · Paging, page tables and TLB

OS31 · Segmentation and contrasts with paging

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS04 · APIs, system calls and virtualization

OS27 · Address binding, dynamic loading and linking

OS30 · Paging, page tables and TLB

OS31 · Segmentation and contrasts with paging

Correct answer: D — Virtualization, in the virtual-machine sense.

OS04 defines virtualization as presenting virtual machine environments and distinguishes it from virtual memory. OS27 describes swapping, OS30 paging and OS31 segmentation as memory-management mechanisms. Under the guest-machine sense used here, virtualization is therefore the option outside that list. The answer depends on that stated sense; the broader phrase memory virtualization would describe a different use of the word.

Evidence in the complete study note

Virtualization presents a virtual machine environment, often allowing guest operating systems on one host through a hypervisor. It differs from virtual memory, which translates a process’s addresses. Kernel, shell, API and VM are not interchangeable labels.OS04
Swapping traditionally moves a whole process to backing store; paging moves individual pages.OS27
Pages and physical frames have equal fixed size P. Divide virtual address v into page=floor(v/P) and offset=v mod P. The page table maps a page to a frame and protection/status flags.OS30
Segmentation uses logical variable-length units such as code, stack and arrays.OS31

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Paging maps fixed-size virtual pages to physical frames and is explicitly a memory-management mechanism.

B · incorrect — Segmentation maps variable-length logical regions and is explicitly a memory-management mechanism.

C · incorrect — Swapping moves a process to backing store and is a memory-management operation described here.

D · correct — In the note’s guest-machine sense, virtualization presents virtual computers rather than naming these process-memory techniques.

Question 45

Which of the following is not a process scheduling algorithm?

  • A. First-Come-First-Serve (FCFS)
  • B. Round-Robin
  • C. Shortest-Job-First (SJF)
  • D. Binary Search Tree (BST)

Source

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 4

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Separate class assignment, movement between queues and the rule for choosing work.

Study-guide sections

OS19 · Multilevel queues and feedback

OS15 · FCFS and the convoy effect

OS16 · SJF/SJN and shortest remaining time

OS18 · Round Robin, quantum and bounded turns

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS19 · Multilevel queues and feedback

OS15 · FCFS and the convoy effect

OS16 · SJF/SJN and shortest remaining time

OS18 · Round Robin, quantum and bounded turns

Correct answer: D — Binary Search Tree is a data structure.

OS19 explicitly states that a binary search tree may implement a queue but is not itself a CPU selection policy. FCFS, Round Robin and SJF are actual scheduling algorithms described in OS15, OS18 and OS16. Consequently BST is the exclusion. Using a tree internally to maintain priorities would not change the selection policy into an algorithm called BST.

Evidence in the complete study note

A binary search tree is a data structure that might implement a queue; it is not itself a CPU selection policy.OS19
FCFS runs ready bursts in arrival order and ordinarily does not preempt.OS15
Non-preemptive SJF/SJN chooses the shortest next burst among currently ready jobs.OS16
RR serves a ready queue cyclically for at most quantum q per turn.OS18

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — FCFS is a scheduling policy that runs ready bursts in their arrival order.

B · incorrect — Round Robin is a scheduling policy assigning cyclic ready-queue turns bounded by a quantum.

C · incorrect — SJF is a scheduling policy selecting the shortest next burst among ready jobs.

D · correct — BST is a data structure that can support queue operations rather than a CPU scheduling policy.

Question 46

Which of the following is not a type of file system?

  • A. FAT32
  • B. NTFS
  • C. EXT4
  • D. HTTP

Source

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS36 · Files, descriptors and ordinary operations

Correct answer: D — HTTP is an application communication protocol.

OS36 explicitly names FAT32, NTFS and ext4 as filesystem formats and contrasts HTTP as an application communication protocol. Filesystems organize stored file content and metadata; a communication protocol does not become a filesystem merely because it transfers file contents. Therefore HTTP is the single option outside the stated filesystem-format category.

Evidence in the complete study note

Sockets also support ordinary read/write, alongside socket/connect/send operations, so read is not uniquely “not a network call.” FAT32, NTFS and ext4 are filesystem formats; HTTP is an application communication protocol.OS36

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — FAT32 is explicitly identified as a filesystem format, making it a member of the requested category.

B · incorrect — NTFS is explicitly identified as a filesystem format, not a communication-protocol exclusion.

C · incorrect — ext4 is explicitly identified as a filesystem format in the complete study note.

D · correct — HTTP is an application communication protocol rather than a filesystem format for organizing stored files.
