D · incorrect — Shells and GUIs provide user interfaces; that is not the bootloader’s principal role here.

Question 54

What is the difference between a kernel-level thread and a user-level thread?

  • A. Kernel-level threads are faster than user-level threads
  • B. Kernel-level threads are managed by the operating system, while user- level threads are managed by the application
  • C. Kernel-level threads can access hardware resources directly, while user-level threads cannot
  • D. There is no difference between a kernel-level thread and a user-level thread

Source

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 13

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare per-thread execution context with shared process resources and the stated thread mapping.

Study-guide sections

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

OS-S02 · Thread management costs and execution privilege

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

OS-S02 · Thread management costs and execution privilege

Correct answer: B — Kernel-level threads are managed by the operating system, while user- level threads are managed by the application

Application management of user threads is explicit on PDF p28; kernel management is explicit on p29. Kernel threads are generally slower to create/manage, and management location does not automatically grant application thread code kernel privilege.

Evidence in the complete study note

User-level threads are managed by a library; kernel threads are known to the OS. In many-to-one mapping, a blocking kernel call can block all user threads and one kernel execution context cannot use multiple CPUs simultaneously. One-to-one permits another kernel thread to run when one blocks. Many-to-many multiplexes user threads over several kernel threads. Speed and reliability depend on implementation; the management layer alone guarantees neither. Application names such as browser or Excel do not prove a particular version’s threading model.OS08
In the conventional library-thread comparison, user-level thread creation and switching can avoid entering the kernel. Kernel-managed thread creation and management commonly incur additional kernel work and are therefore generally slower to create and manage in this comparison. This is a typical comparison, not a universal speed or reliability guarantee.OS-S02
Being scheduled or managed by the kernel does not give application thread code kernel privilege. Ordinary applications and supporting utilities execute in restricted user mode and request protected services through libraries and system calls. Kernel component code executes in privileged kernel mode and performs permitted low-level hardware operations.OS-S02

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Opposes the p29 slower-to-create/manage comparison.

B · correct — Exactly matches management location on pp28–29.

C · incorrect — Confuses scheduling/management by the kernel with execution privilege; p53 distinguishes user code from kernel code.

D · incorrect — Management location and costs explicitly differ.

Question 55

Which listed item is a general data structure rather than a deadlock-specific avoidance method or dependency-graph analysis?

  • A. Banker's algorithm
  • B. Wait-for graph algorithm
  • C. Resource allocation graph algorithm
  • D. Binary search tree algorithm

Adapted from

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 14

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compute remaining needs and simulate feasible completions before deciding whether an allocation is safe.

Study-guide sections

OS25 · Avoidance and the Banker safety test

OS23 · Resource graphs and missing-diagram limits

OS19 · Multilevel queues and feedback

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS25 · Avoidance and the Banker safety test

OS23 · Resource graphs and missing-diagram limits

OS19 · Multilevel queues and feedback

Correct answer: D — Binary search tree algorithm

Banker’s algorithm checks whether tentative allocations leave a safe completion sequence and is an avoidance method. Wait-for and resource-allocation graphs represent dependencies used in deadlock reasoning. A binary search tree is a general data structure and does not, by its name alone, specify deadlock handling. The broader category distinguishes the unrelated item without misclassifying avoidance or detection as strict prevention.

Evidence in the complete study note

Avoidance allows requests only if the resulting state remains safe. Banker requires maximum claims. Compute Need=Max−Allocation.OS25
In a resource-allocation graph, P→R is a request and an instance R→P is an assignment. Follow these directions to form waits. No cycle implies no deadlock; with one instance of every resource type, a cycle implies deadlock. With multiple instances a cycle alone is insufficient: another instance may permit completion. A wait-for graph replaces single-instance resource nodes with P→Q when P waits for Q.OS23
A binary search tree is a data structure that might implement a queue; it is not itself a CPU selection policy.OS19

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Banker is a deadlock-avoidance method, so it belongs to the stated handling category.

B · incorrect — A wait-for graph directly represents process dependencies for deadlock analysis.

C · incorrect — A resource-allocation graph represents resource requests and assignments for deadlock reasoning.

D · correct — A binary search tree is a general search structure rather than a deadlock-specific method.

Question 56

What is the general execution role of a process in an operating system, regardless of the application it runs?

  • A. To manage user accounts
  • B. To manage hardware resources
  • C. To execute a program instance with its own address space and resources
  • D. To provide a user interface

Adapted from

Distinguish stored instructions, an executing instance and its saved execution state.

Study-guide sections

OS05 · Programs, processes and PCB contents

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS05 · Programs, processes and PCB contents

OS01 · Objectives, resource management and evolution

Correct answer: C — To execute a program instance with its own address space and resources

A process is an executing instance of a program, accompanied by an address space and resources. Its application may administer accounts, interact with hardware through services, or display a user interface, but none of those specialized jobs defines every process. The execution option expresses the general role. It also distinguishes a running instance from a program stored as instructions.

Evidence in the complete study note

A program is stored instructions; a process is an executing instance with address space and resources. A process does not universally “manage applications” or “provide a user interface.” The PCB records identity, state, PC/registers, scheduling information, memory/accounting details and I/O/open-resource state.OS05
An OS allocates CPU time, memory, storage, devices and communication resources, and provides services to programs.OS01

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Account administration is only one possible application responsibility.

B · incorrect — Hardware management is an OS responsibility rather than the general role of every process.

C · correct — Executing a program instance with an address space and resources is the process abstraction.

D · incorrect — Many processes run without providing a user interface.

Question 57

Which of the following is not a type of process state in an operating system?

  • A. Ready
  • B. Running
  • C. Blocked
  • D. Sleeping Beauty

Source

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 16

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Decide whether the process is eligible, running or waiting, then identify the responsible queue or scheduling role.

Study-guide sections

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Correct answer: D — Sleeping Beauty

The state list on PDF p16 contains Ready, Running and Waiting. Interpret Blocked as the waiting-for-event state. Sleeping Beauty is not one of this module’s five named states.

Evidence in the complete study note

New processes are created/admitted; ready means eligible for CPU but not currently running. Running uses a CPU. Waiting/blocked means unable to proceed until an event; terminated means execution has ended. Running→ready follows time-slice expiry or another preemption. Running→waiting follows blocking I/O. I/O completion normally gives waiting→ready, not a guaranteed immediate running state.OS06

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Ready is explicitly listed.

B · incorrect — Running is explicitly listed.

C · incorrect — Blocked is the conventional paraphrase of waiting for an event; Waiting is the module’s exact term.

D · correct — Not present in the five-state model, and not a defined state term.

Question 58

What is the difference between a process and a thread in an operating system?

  • A. A process is a unit of execution, while a thread is a unit of scheduling
  • B. A process is a unit of scheduling, while a thread is a unit of execution
  • C. There is no difference between a process and a thread
  • D. A process can have multiple threads, while a thread can only belong to one process

Source

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 17

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare per-thread execution context with shared process resources and the stated thread mapping.

Study-guide sections

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

Correct answer: D — A process can have multiple threads, while a thread can only belong to one process

PDF p27 says every thread belongs to exactly one process and separate flows improve parallel execution; p29 supports multiple threads within one process. D directly expresses that relationship.

Evidence in the complete study note

A thread has its own PC, registers and stack. Threads in a process share code, address space and usually open-file resources. A process can contain many threads; a thread belongs to one process at a time. Threads are not a group of processes and need not execute sequentially. Independent processes can exist; cooperating ones communicate/share data.OS08
User-level threads are managed by a library; kernel threads are known to the OS. In many-to-one mapping, a blocking kernel call can block all user threads and one kernel execution context cannot use multiple CPUs simultaneously. One-to-one permits another kernel thread to run when one blocks. Many-to-many multiplexes user threads over several kernel threads.OS08

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Processes and threads can both be scheduled (pp20,29); this is not an exclusive distinction.

B · incorrect — Both may participate in scheduling and execution; reversal does not define their containment.

C · incorrect — PDF p27 explicitly distinguishes heavy processes from light threads.

D · correct — Directly follows the one-process-per-thread and multithreaded-process definitions (pp27,29).

Question 59

Which of the following is not a type of synchronization mechanism used in operating systems?

  • A. Mutex
  • B. Semaphore
  • C. Monitor
  • D. Keyboard

Source

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 18

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Track available permits and blocked operations using the stated semaphore model.

Study-guide sections

OS11 · Semaphores and resource-count traces

OS10 · Races, critical sections and correctness

OS34 · Device controllers, drivers and I/O services

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS11 · Semaphores and resource-count traces

OS10 · Races, critical sections and correctness

OS34 · Device controllers, drivers and I/O services

Correct answer: D — Keyboard.

OS10 describes monitors as protected operations with condition synchronization, and OS11 explains mutex/semaphore exclusion mechanisms. These coordinate concurrent execution. A keyboard is an input device rather than such a coordination abstraction. Interrupts generated by hardware may participate in event handling, but that does not make the keyboard itself a mutex, semaphore or monitor mechanism.

Evidence in the complete study note

A semaphore provides atomic wait/P/down and signal/V/up operations. Under the nonnegative-token model, wait consumes one available token or blocks when none is available; signal supplies a token and may wake a waiter. Implementations using negative internal counts encode waiters differently, so state the model. A binary semaphore represents one permit; a counting semaphore tracks several. A mutex normally has ownership rules that a general semaphore lacks.OS11
A barrier waits until a defined set of participants reaches a point; a monitor encapsulates protected operations with condition synchronization.OS10
Input/output/storage/NIC devices are OS-managed hardware; user accounts are identities.OS34

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A mutex provides owned mutual exclusion and is a synchronization mechanism in the note.

B · incorrect — A semaphore coordinates participants through atomic wait and signal operations, so it is a synchronization mechanism.

C · incorrect — A monitor combines protected operations and condition synchronization, making it a genuine synchronization abstraction.

D · correct — A keyboard is a physical input device rather than a process/thread synchronization abstraction.

Question 61

Which item is an application-layer protocol rather than a primitive OS IPC mechanism such as a stream, shared region or message queue?

  • A. Pipes
  • B. Shared memory
  • C. Message queues
  • D. HTTP requests

Adapted from

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 20

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify how data moves, which participant waits and what event allows the operation to continue.

Study-guide sections

OS09 · IPC and blocking semantics

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS09 · IPC and blocking semantics

Correct answer: D — HTTP requests

Pipes provide streams, shared memory provides a common region, and message queues preserve messages. These are primitive IPC mechanisms in the stated classification. HTTP is an application protocol that can use an underlying communication mechanism such as sockets. It can certainly carry interprocess communication, but it is the only listed application-layer protocol rather than one of the primitive mechanisms requested here.

Evidence in the complete study note

Shared memory lets processes access a common region and requires synchronization for conflicting updates. Message passing exchanges data through send/receive operations. Pipes provide streams; message queues preserve messages; sockets connect endpoints; RPC requests a remote operation. HTTP is an application protocol over a communication mechanism and can carry interprocess communication, so it is not universally “not IPC.”OS09

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Pipes are primitive stream-based IPC mechanisms.

B · incorrect — Shared memory is a primitive common-region IPC mechanism.

C · incorrect — Message queues are primitive message-preserving IPC mechanisms.

D · correct — HTTP requests use an application-layer protocol over an underlying mechanism.

Question 62

What is the general purpose of a system call in an operating system?

  • A. To manage user accounts
  • B. To request a protected kernel service
  • C. To manage software applications
  • D. To provide a user interface

Adapted from

Study-guide sections

OS04 · APIs, system calls and virtualization

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS04 · APIs, system calls and virtualization

Correct answer: B — To request a protected kernel service

A system call is the mechanism through which executing code requests a protected kernel service. Process creation, file reads and socket operations are examples. A program that manages accounts, applications or a user interface may use system calls, but those tasks are not the mechanism’s general purpose. The option captures the common role without restricting it to one kind of resource.

Evidence in the complete study note

A system call requests a protected kernel service, such as creating a process, reading a file or opening a socket. A user-facing API/library may wrap that request; not every library function enters the kernel.OS04

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Account management can use system calls but does not define their general purpose.

B · correct — Requesting a protected kernel service states the general purpose.

C · incorrect — Managing applications is a possible use rather than the system-call mechanism’s definition.

D · incorrect — A UI may issue calls, but providing a UI is not the general system-call role.

Question 63

Which of the following is not a type of scheduling policy used in operating systems?

  • A. Preemptive
  • B. Non-preemptive
  • C. Cooperative
  • D. Infinite

Source

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 23

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Check whether the running burst can be interrupted before completion or blocking.

Study-guide sections

OS14 · Preemptive versus non-preemptive decisions

OS19 · Multilevel queues and feedback

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS14 · Preemptive versus non-preemptive decisions

OS19 · Multilevel queues and feedback

Correct answer: D — Infinite is not a scheduling-policy category.

OS14 distinguishes preemptive and non-preemptive scheduling and explains cooperative scheduling as relying on voluntary release. OS19 explicitly rejects Infinite as a standard scheduling-policy category. Thus the first three labels describe genuine scheduling approaches, whereas infinite does not identify a selection or preemption rule. It should not be confused with a possible unbounded waiting-time outcome.

Evidence in the complete study note

Non-preemptive scheduling keeps a running CPU burst until completion, blocking or voluntary yielding. Preemptive scheduling can return running work to ready, usually after a timer quantum or a more urgent arrival. FCFS and non-preemptive SJF are non-preemptive; RR and SRTF are preemptive. Priority can be either, so its name alone cannot decide. Cooperative describes relying on voluntary release.OS14
“Infinite” is not a standard scheduling-policy category.OS19

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Preemptive scheduling can interrupt running work and return it to the ready state.

B · incorrect — Non-preemptive scheduling retains a running burst until completion, blocking or voluntary release.

C · incorrect — Cooperative scheduling is the recognized approach relying on voluntary release of execution.

D · correct — Infinite names no scheduling selection or preemption policy in the provided note.

Question 64

24.What is the purpose of a page table in memory management?

  • A. To translate virtual addresses to physical addresses
  • B. To manage user accounts
  • C. To manage software applications
  • D. To provide a user interface

Source

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 24

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Split the address into page and offset, then apply the mapping and access checks.

Study-guide sections

OS30 · Paging, page tables and TLB

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS30 · Paging, page tables and TLB

Correct answer: A — To translate virtual addresses to physical addresses

Page number indexes a table containing physical page bases (PDF p34); combining the base with the page offset yields the physical address (p35). Thus a page table participates in virtual-to-physical translation, A.

Evidence in the complete study note

Pages and physical frames have equal fixed size P. Divide virtual address v into page=floor(v/P) and offset=v mod P. The page table maps a page to a frame and protection/status flags. Physical address=frame×P+offset. A TLB caches translations; a TLB miss may require a table walk while the page is already resident.OS30

Choice by choice

A · correct — Matches page-table address lookup (pp34–35).

B · incorrect — User accounts are not the page-number-to-frame mapping.

C · incorrect — Application management is broader than the translation data structure.

D · incorrect — An execution address map does not provide the user interface.

Question 68

Which item is a hardware/CPU event-notification mechanism rather than a high-level lock, participant rendezvous, or protected-operation abstraction?

  • A. Locks
  • B. Barriers
  • C. Monitors
  • D. Interrupts

Adapted from

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 29

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify conflicting shared accesses and the safety or progress property required.

Study-guide sections

OS10 · Races, critical sections and correctness

OS11 · Semaphores and resource-count traces

OS34 · Device controllers, drivers and I/O services

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS10 · Races, critical sections and correctness

OS11 · Semaphores and resource-count traces

OS34 · Device controllers, drivers and I/O services

Correct answer: D — Interrupts

Locks coordinate entry to protected state, barriers wait for participants to reach a common point, and monitors encapsulate protected operations with conditions. Interrupts notify the CPU of events and may help coordinate work, but belong to a different abstraction level. The classification therefore selects interrupts without denying that interrupts can participate in synchronization or completion notification.

Evidence in the complete study note

Keep critical regions small and ensure every conflicting access uses the same protection. A barrier waits until a defined set of participants reaches a point; a monitor encapsulates protected operations with condition synchronization.OS10
A mutex normally has ownership rules that a general semaphore lacks.OS11
Polling checks readiness repeatedly; interrupts request CPU attention; DMA transfers blocks after setup without CPU work for every byte.OS34

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A lock is a high-level mutual-exclusion abstraction.

B · incorrect — A barrier is a participant rendezvous abstraction.

C · incorrect — A monitor encapsulates protected operations and condition synchronization.

D · correct — An interrupt is a CPU event-notification mechanism, the different abstraction level requested.

Question 70

Which item coordinates access but does not itself transfer arbitrary data payloads or request an operation in another process?

  • A. Message passing
  • B. Shared memory
  • C. Remote procedure call (RPC)
  • D. File locking

Adapted from

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 31

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify how data moves, which participant waits and what event allows the operation to continue.

Study-guide sections

OS09 · IPC and blocking semantics

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS09 · IPC and blocking semantics

Correct answer: D — File locking

Message passing transfers messages, shared memory carries data through a common region, and RPC requests an operation in another process or machine. File locking instead coordinates who may access a file or region. Locks can therefore be part of IPC synchronization while not being a payload-transfer mechanism themselves. The narrower wording makes file locking the unique match without denying its coordination role.

Evidence in the complete study note

Shared memory lets processes access a common region and requires synchronization for conflicting updates. Message passing exchanges data through send/receive operations. Pipes provide streams; message queues preserve messages; sockets connect endpoints; RPC requests a remote operation.OS09
File locks coordinate access rather than transfer arbitrary payloads, though locking is synchronization within IPC.OS09

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Message passing transfers data using send/receive operations.

B · incorrect — Shared memory carries data in a common accessible region.

C · incorrect — RPC communicates a request to perform another process’s operation.

D · correct — File locking coordinates access rather than carrying arbitrary payloads itself.

Question 72

In this question only, “task” means a thread within a process. Which statement describes the process–task ownership relationship?

  • A. There is no difference between a process and a task
  • B. A process is a unit of execution, while a task is a unit of scheduling
  • C. A process can have multiple tasks, while a task can only belong to one process
  • D. A task is a unit of execution, while a process is a unit of scheduling

Adapted from

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 33

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare per-thread execution context with shared process resources and the stated thread mapping.

Study-guide sections

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

OS05 · Programs, processes and PCB contents

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

OS05 · Programs, processes and PCB contents

Correct answer: C — A process can have multiple tasks, while a task can only belong to one process

With task explicitly defined to mean thread, a process may contain several tasks while each task belongs to one process at a time. The tasks share the process address space but have their own execution context. This ownership relationship does not follow from the word task universally; it follows from the definition supplied here. Execution-versus-scheduling slogans do not state that relationship.

Evidence in the complete study note

A thread has its own PC, registers and stack. Threads in a process share code, address space and usually open-file resources. A process can contain many threads; a thread belongs to one process at a time. Threads are not a group of processes and need not execute sequentially. Independent processes can exist; cooperating ones communicate/share data.OS08
Task is OS-specific terminology: it may mean process, thread or schedulable entity.OS05

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A thread/task is not identical to its containing process under this definition.

B · incorrect — The execution-versus-scheduling description does not express the defined ownership relationship.

C · correct — A process can contain multiple threads/tasks, each belonging to one process at a time.

D · incorrect — The reversed execution/scheduling labels do not describe thread ownership.

Question 73

Which of the following is not a type of memory allocation algorithm used in operating systems?

  • A. First-Fit
  • B. Best-Fit
  • C. Next-Fit
  • D. Binary-Fit

Source

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 34

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Apply the stated hole-selection or base-and-bound rule with its explicit convention.

Study-guide sections

OS28 · Contiguous allocation and fit policies

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS28 · Contiguous allocation and fit policies

Correct answer: D — Binary-Fit is not one of the standard fit-policy names.

OS28 defines first-fit, next-fit, best-fit and worst-fit, then explicitly says binary-fit is not one of these standard names. First-fit, best-fit and next-fit therefore belong to the allocation-policy category used by the question. Binary-fit is the supported exclusion; it should not be invented as a synonym for another algorithm merely because the word binary is familiar.

Evidence in the complete study note

First-fit selects the first adequate hole; next-fit continues from the previous search position; best-fit selects the smallest adequate hole; worst-fit selects the largest. No policy universally optimizes both search time and future storage utilization. Binary-fit is not one of these standard names.OS28

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — First-fit selects the first adequate hole and is an explicitly defined memory-allocation policy.

B · incorrect — Best-fit chooses the smallest adequate hole and is an explicitly defined memory-allocation policy.

C · incorrect — Next-fit resumes search from the previous position and is an explicitly defined allocation policy.

D · correct — The note explicitly excludes binary-fit from the standard memory-allocation fit-policy names.

Question 74

What is the purpose of a system call interface in an operating system?

  • A. To manage user accounts
  • B. To manage hardware resources
  • C. To manage software applications
  • D. To provide a standardized interface for application programs to make system calls

Identify the abstraction layer and separate a protected service request from an application task.

Study-guide sections

OS04 · APIs, system calls and virtualization

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS04 · APIs, system calls and virtualization

Correct answer: D — Provide the interface through which applications request system calls.

OS04 defines a system call as requesting a protected kernel service and distinguishes an API/library wrapper from the direct boundary. Option D describes the interface that applications use to make those requests. Account, hardware and application management can be services reached through it; they are not the general purpose of the interface itself.

Evidence in the complete study note

A system call requests a protected kernel service, such as creating a process, reading a file or opening a socket. A user-facing API/library may wrap that request; not every library function enters the kernel. Distinguish the application API from the direct user/kernel boundary when describing access to OS services.OS04

Choice by choice

B · incorrect — Hardware operations may be requested through services; general hardware management is not the interface definition.

C · incorrect — Application management is a possible service use rather than the purpose of the syscall interface.

D · correct — The interface supplies the application-to-system-call request path for protected kernel services.

Question 75

Question 78

Which listed operation can receive bytes from a socket while also serving as a generic file-input operation in the descriptor model?

  • A. socket()
  • B. connect()
  • C. read()
  • D. send()

Adapted from

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 40

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish a file operation, a resource handle, an offset and file metadata.

Study-guide sections

OS36 · Files, descriptors and ordinary operations

OS-S04 · Communication endpoints and timed I/O events

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS36 · Files, descriptors and ordinary operations

OS-S04 · Communication endpoints and timed I/O events

Correct answer: C — read()

Sockets can support the ordinary read/write interface as well as specialized socket operations. A read therefore need not be confined to disk files: it can receive bytes from a socket descriptor. socket creates an endpoint, connect establishes a connection, and send transmits data. Only read matches both the generic file-input role and the ability to receive socket data in this model.

Evidence in the complete study note

Sockets also support ordinary read/write, alongside socket/connect/send operations, so read is not uniquely “not a network call.” FAT32, NTFS and ext4 are filesystem formats; HTTP is an application communication protocol.OS36
In the descriptor-based socket API, socket() creates a communication endpoint and returns its descriptor. connect() associates the socket with the requested peer address; send() transmits data to another socket. Receiving through read() uses the ordinary descriptor-input interface described in the file-services lesson.OS-S04

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — socket creates a communication endpoint rather than reading bytes from it.

B · incorrect — connect establishes a connection rather than receiving its data.

C · correct — read is generic descriptor input and can receive bytes from a socket.

D · incorrect — send transmits data rather than performing the requested input operation.

Question 79

What is the purpose of a system clock in an operating system?

  • A. To manage user accounts
  • B. To manage hardware resources
  • C. To manage software applications
  • D. To provide a reference for measuring time and scheduling processes and events

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 41

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS35 · Buffering, caching, spooling and clocks

OS-S04 · Communication endpoints and timed I/O events

Correct answer: D — To provide a reference for measuring time and scheduling processes and events

The clock driver maintains time and decrements the quantum counter to invoke the scheduler (PDF p46, section 12.3 Clocks). D is the matching time-and-scheduling purpose.

Evidence in the complete study note

The clock/timer supports timekeeping, timeouts, accounting and preemptive scheduling.OS35
IRQ means interrupt request. A controller can raise an interrupt to request CPU attention. In a clock-tick scheduler, the scheduler initializes a running process’s quantum counter; each clock interrupt decrements it, and reaching zero triggers scheduling. Clock support also maintains time of day and usage accounting.OS-S04

Choice by choice

B · incorrect — Generic resource management omits the clock’s time reference.

C · incorrect — Software management is not its stated task.

D · correct — Time of day and quantum expiration are both clock-driver functions on p46.

Question 81

What is the purpose of a file descriptor in an operating system?

  • A. To manage user accounts
  • B. To manage hardware resources
  • C. To manage software applications
  • D. To provide a reference to an open file or communication endpoint

Source

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 43

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish a file operation, a resource handle, an offset and file metadata.

Study-guide sections

OS36 · Files, descriptors and ordinary operations

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS36 · Files, descriptors and ordinary operations

Correct answer: D — A process-local reference to an open file or communication endpoint.

OS36 defines a file descriptor as a process-local integer handle and separately places offset and access mode in associated open-file state. It also describes sockets supporting ordinary descriptor I/O. The descriptor therefore refers to an open resource or endpoint; it is not an account manager, hardware manager or application manager. D is the specific role matching that definition.

Evidence in the complete study note

A file has content and metadata such as name, size, owner, permissions and timestamps. open obtains an open-file reference, read/write transfer bytes, seek changes an offset, close releases a reference, truncate changes size and unlink removes a name. A file descriptor is a process-local integer handle; the associated open-file state contains the current offset and access mode.OS36
Sockets also support ordinary read/write, alongside socket/connect/send operations, so read is not uniquely “not a network call.” FAT32, NTFS and ext4 are filesystem formats; HTTP is an application communication protocol.OS36

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — A descriptor identifies an open resource within a process, rather than managing user accounts.

B · incorrect — A descriptor is a handle used to access a resource, not a general hardware-management component.

C · incorrect — Descriptors support resource I/O and do not manage application lifecycles or software installations.

D · correct — An open-file or socket reference is the resource-handle purpose of a file descriptor.

Question 90

Using the first four generations described in NYU’s OS-history notes (vacuum tubes; transistors; integrated circuits; personal computers), which generation is associated with resident uniprogrammed batch operating systems, following the manual/no-resident-OS era?

  • C. Third Generation

Compare the stated historical clues and distinguish origin, characteristic era and technical capability.

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS-S01 · UNIX origins and the first four historical generations

Correct answer: B — Second Generation

The first four generations in NYU’s historical taxonomy associates second-generation transistor machines with uniprogrammed batch systems whose OS remains resident between jobs. The first era has no separate resident OS; the third emphasizes ICs and multiprogramming, and the fourth personal computers. OS02 explains batch operation, while the generation mapping is supplied by the supplementary university notes. This is a scoped teaching convention rather than a universal first-OS claim.

Evidence in the complete study note

In the first four generations of the cited NYU teaching taxonomy, the first generation uses vacuum tubes and manual single-user operation without a separate resident OS. The second generation pairs transistors with uniprogrammed batch systems; the resident OS starts successive jobs without being reloaded for every job.OS-S01
The third generation pairs integrated circuits with multiprogramming. The fourth emphasizes personal computers. These are characteristic-era associations, not claims that each feature existed exclusively in one era.OS-S01

The teaching taxonomy places no separate OS in the first era, resident uniprogrammed batch systems in the second, ICs/multiprogramming in the third and personal computers in the fourth.

Question 91

What is the primary function of a kernel in an operating system?

Question 96

During a conventional disk-based boot, the bootloader reads the kernel image from disk. Into which main execution-memory region is it loaded before control is transferred to it?

  • A. RAM
  • B. Hard disk
  • C. CPU cache
  • D. ROM

Adapted from

  • Operating Systems practice questions · Question 28

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Distinguish command interfaces, privileged core services and the stages of booting.

Study-guide sections

OS03 · Kernel, shell, privilege and boot

OS35 · Buffering, caching, spooling and clocks

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS03 · Kernel, shell, privilege and boot

OS35 · Buffering, caching, spooling and clocks

Correct answer: A — RAM

In the stated boot sequence, firmware and bootloader stages run before control passes to the kernel loaded into RAM. The disk supplies the stored image, while RAM is its main execution-memory destination. CPU cache can retain copies of memory contents for faster reuse, but that is distinct from the image’s main-memory loading location. ROM is not the RAM destination specified by this conventional boot model.

Evidence in the complete study note

Firmware/bootloader stages execute before transferring control to a kernel loaded into RAM.OS03
Caching retains copies to accelerate reuse.OS35

Choice by choice

A · correct — RAM is the main execution-memory destination for the loaded kernel image in the stated boot sequence.

B · incorrect — The hard disk is the source of the stored image here, not its main-memory loading destination.

C · incorrect — Cache retains copies for reuse; it is not the bootloader’s main-memory image region.

D · incorrect — ROM does not identify the writable RAM destination of the kernel image in this stated disk-boot model.

Question 101

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Question 102

Under preemptive priority scheduling, which event sends a currently running, unfinished process back to the ready state?

  • A. Arrival of a higher-priority ready process that preempts it
  • B. Completion of its final CPU burst
  • C. Suspension while waiting for an event
  • D. Issuing a blocking I/O request

Adapted from

  • Mock exam 1 · Question 23

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Decide whether the process is eligible, running or waiting, then identify the responsible queue or scheduling role.

Study-guide sections

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

Correct answer: A — Arrival of a higher-priority ready process that preempts it

Preemption removes the CPU from a running process that can still execute, returning it to ready. In the stated priority policy, arrival of a higher-priority ready process triggers that handoff. Final completion ends execution, while a blocking I/O request makes the process wait for an event. Suspension while waiting also does not describe ordinary readiness, so only the explicit preemption event fits.

Evidence in the complete study note

New processes are created/admitted; ready means eligible for CPU but not currently running. Running uses a CPU. Waiting/blocked means unable to proceed until an event; terminated means execution has ended. Running→ready follows time-slice expiry or another preemption. Running→waiting follows blocking I/O. I/O completion normally gives waiting→ready, not a guaranteed immediate running state.OS06
Priority scheduling chooses the highest-priority ready job. Check whether a smaller or larger numeric value means higher priority. State whether a high-priority arrival preempts.OS17

Choice by choice

A · correct — The higher-priority arrival preempts the runnable process and returns it to ready.

B · incorrect — Final completion transitions to terminated, not ready.

C · incorrect — Suspended waiting work is not eligible for the ordinary ready queue.

D · incorrect — Blocking I/O transitions running to waiting.

Question 103

One of the following deadlock handling methods is used if it occurs rarely and is insignificant.

  • A. Prevention
  • B. waiting
  • C. recovery
  • D. Ignorance

Adapted from

  • Mock exam 1 · Question 24

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Separate handling before allocation from actions taken after detection, and consider the cost of checking.

Study-guide sections

OS26 · Detection, recovery and intentional ignoring

OS24 · Prevention: identify the condition removed

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS26 · Detection, recovery and intentional ignoring

OS24 · Prevention: identify the condition removed

Correct answer: D — Ignorance, the intentional ignoring/ostrich policy.

The ostrich or ignoring policy accepts the risk of a sufficiently rare, insignificant deadlock problem when systematic handling is not worthwhile. That is the intended meaning of Ignorance. Prevention instead constrains requests to break a necessary deadlock condition, and recovery acts after detection. Merely waiting is not the same as deliberately choosing to tolerate the rare problem.

Evidence in the complete study note

Ignoring a sufficiently rare problem is sometimes called the ostrich policy, but real systems still prevent or detect selected deadlocks. “Deadlock acceptance” can mean intentional ignoring, not necessarily an invalid approach.OS26
Prevention constrains how requests are made. Share a resource where safe to remove mutual exclusion; request all needed resources before holding any to remove hold-and-wait; permit safe resource withdrawal/restart to attack no-preemption; impose a global acquisition order to remove circular wait.OS24

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Prevention constrains resource requests to remove a necessary deadlock condition; it does not deliberately ignore the problem.

B · incorrect — Waiting describes a process behavior, not the deliberate policy of accepting rare deadlocks.

C · incorrect — Recovery takes action after deadlock detection rather than choosing to ignore the rare problem.

D · correct — Ignorance names the intentional ignoring or ostrich policy in this context.

Question 104

One of the following is false about linked allocation.

  • A. Is based on sequential file access
  • B. Use pointer concept
  • C. No external fragmentation
  • D. The size of the file should be set in advanced

Source

  • Mock exam 3 · Question 19

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace how logical file blocks reach physical blocks and account for pointer metadata.

Study-guide sections

OS38 · Contiguous, linked and indexed file allocation

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS38 · Contiguous, linked and indexed file allocation

Correct answer: D — The size of the file should be set in advanced

PDF p49 says linked allocation carries links to disk blocks, is effective for sequential access and has no external fragmentation. No advance contiguous size reservation is part of that linked structure; D confuses it with a fixed extent requirement.

Evidence in the complete study note

Contiguous allocation uses consecutive blocks; logical block i is start+i and supports direct access, but growth and external fragmentation are problems. Extents allow several contiguous runs. Linked allocation chains blocks and supports growth without reserving a complete contiguous file; locating block i requires traversing preceding links. FAT stores chains in a table; caching that table does not change the chain into a direct arithmetic mapping.OS38

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Sequential access effectiveness is explicitly stated on p49.

B · incorrect — Links/pointers are explicitly stated on p49.

C · incorrect — No external fragmentation is explicitly stated on p49.

D · correct — A chain can link another block without requiring one pre-sized contiguous extent; no advance-size requirement follows from the p49 linked structure.

Question 105

Consider three specified implementations: an Excel application process with several worker threads, a client-browser process with multiple threads sharing its address space, and a server process using only its single main thread. Which listed process does not use multithreading?

  • A. Excel application
  • B. Client browser
  • C. Server
  • D. None of the choices is correct

Study-guide sections

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

Correct answer: C — Server

Multithreading means that one process contains multiple threads of execution. The described spreadsheet and browser processes each have several threads sharing their process resources. The specified server implementation has only its main thread and therefore is not multithreaded. This conclusion follows from the supplied designs, not from a universal claim about every version of Excel, every browser, or every server.

Evidence in the complete study note

A thread has its own PC, registers and stack. Threads in a process share code, address space and usually open-file resources. A process can contain many threads; a thread belongs to one process at a time. Threads are not a group of processes and need not execute sequentially.OS08

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — The specified Excel process has several worker threads and is multithreaded.

B · incorrect — The specified browser process has multiple threads and is multithreaded.

C · correct — The specified server has only one thread and is not multithreaded.

D · incorrect — C is a valid non-multithreaded implementation, so none is incorrect.

Question 106

One of the following security techniques is not supported by modern operating systems.

  • A. Cryptography
  • B. Authentication
  • C. Firewall
  • D. None of the above

Distinguish identity checks, permission checks and the particular security property at issue.

Study-guide sections

OS39 · Protection, authentication and threats

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS39 · Protection, authentication and threats

Correct answer: D — None: modern OS families can support all three techniques.

OS39 states that modern OS families can support cryptography, authentication and firewalls, while support/configuration depends on product. Therefore none of the three is categorically unsupported by modern operating systems. The answer is a capability-category statement, not a guarantee that every installation includes every feature or enables it by default. Each technique has a distinct security role.

Evidence in the complete study note

Password hashing verifies secrets without storing plaintext; one-time passwords expire after use. Encryption protects confidentiality under correct key use but does not automatically provide integrity. Trojans masquerade as useful software, worms propagate independently and viruses attach to hosts; denial of service harms availability. Modern OS families can support cryptography, authentication and firewalls, but support/configuration depends on product.OS39

Choice by choice

D · correct — All three techniques can be supported; none is categorically outside modern operating-system security capabilities.

Question 107

Under the basic fixed-page and variable-length-segment memory models, which listed differences are correct? Select all that apply.

  • A. Paging leads to internal fragmentation whereas segmentation leads external fragmentation
  • B. The size of block is fixed in the case of paging but variable in the case of segmentation
  • C. Paging use page table whereas segmentation use segmentation table
  • D. Paging is visible to user whereas segmentation is invisible to user

Adapted from

  • Mock exam 2 · Question 16

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Check the logical region and offset bound before computing its physical location.

Study-guide sections

OS31 · Segmentation and contrasts with paging

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS31 · Segmentation and contrasts with paging

Correct answers (select all): A, B, C — Paging leads to internal fragmentation whereas segmentation leads external fragmentation; The size of block is fixed in the case of paging but variable in the case of segmentation; Paging use page table whereas segmentation use segmentation table

Basic paging uses fixed-size pages and page tables, and its final allocated page can contain unused space. Basic segmentation uses variable-length logical units and segment tables, so free holes can cause external fragmentation. Those properties make A, B and C valid contrasts. D reverses the usual abstraction relationship: segments reflect program structure, while ordinary page placement is largely transparent to the application.

Evidence in the complete study note

Under the basic models, paging uses fixed units/page tables and can waste space internally; segmentation uses variable logical units/segment tables and can fragment externally. Segments reflect program structure whereas ordinary pages are largely transparent to applications. Apply all three contrasts when comparing paging and segmentation.OS31

Choice by choice

A · correct — Paging can waste space internally; variable-length segmentation can fragment externally.

B · correct — Pages have a fixed size while segments have variable lengths.

C · correct — Paging uses page tables and segmentation uses segment tables.

D · incorrect — Segments reflect program structure; ordinary pages are largely transparent, reversing this option.

Question 108

One of the following IO services is used store data in memory while transferring between devices.

  • A. Buffering
  • B. Scheduling
  • C. Caching
  • D. Spooling

Source

  • Mock exam 2 · Question 17

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Identify whether data is in transit, retained for reuse or queued for a device.

Study-guide sections

OS35 · Buffering, caching, spooling and clocks

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS35 · Buffering, caching, spooling and clocks

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Correct answer: A — Buffering

PDF p47 defines buffering as retaining data while transferred between devices or an application and device. This is A; caching stores reusable copies and spooling queues a noninterleavable output stream.

Evidence in the complete study note

Buffering temporarily holds data in transit to absorb speed/transfer-size differences. Caching retains copies to accelerate reuse. Spooling queues jobs for a device such as a printer, usually on disk, so producers need not own it throughout job creation. These have different purposes even if one implementation combines them.OS35
Long-term scheduling admits jobs; medium-term scheduling suspends/swaps them; short-term scheduling selects ready work.OS06

Choice by choice

A · correct — Directly matches transfer-time storage (p47).

B · incorrect — Scheduling reorders requests, rather than providing transfer storage.

C · incorrect — Caching keeps copies for more efficient access, not specifically transfer mismatch handling.

D · incorrect — Spooling holds queued device output, a specialized buffer use; no printer/output serialization is specified in the stem.

Question 109

Which directory structure describes the commonly presented namespace of modern general-purpose operating systems, with nested directories and absolute or relative paths? Choose the structural category, not every simpler arrangement that might also be implemented.

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS37 · Directories, mounting and links

Correct answer: A — multi-level

A multilevel or tree namespace supports nested directories and paths that can be absolute or relative. That is the structure the notes identify as commonly presented by modern general-purpose systems. A two-level structure separates users but does not express arbitrary nesting, and a single-level structure has only one namespace level. The aggregate therefore includes structures that do not match the requested hierarchical category.

Evidence in the complete study note

A single-level directory requires unique names in one namespace. Two-level directories separate users. A tree allows nested directories and absolute/relative paths; links can create shared graphs. Modern general-purpose systems commonly present hierarchical namespaces, but a feature name alone does not prove that every system excludes simpler arrangements.OS37

B · incorrect — Single-level and two-level arrangements do not both describe the requested nested tree, so all is incorrect.

C · incorrect — Two-level separates users without providing the general nested hierarchy asked for.

D · incorrect — Single-level has no nested directory hierarchy.

Question 111

Which one of the following is asynchronous in message communication?

  • A. Non-block receive
  • B. Blocking receive
  • C. Blocking send
  • D. Direct message

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · Question 1-2

Identify how data moves, which participant waits and what event allows the operation to continue.

Study-guide sections

OS09 · IPC and blocking semantics

OS-S04 · Communication endpoints and timed I/O events

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS09 · IPC and blocking semantics

OS-S04 · Communication endpoints and timed I/O events

Correct answer: A — Nonblocking receive.

OS09 defines nonblocking operations as returning promptly with data, progress or a would-block result. Among the options, nonblocking receive therefore supplies the asynchronous/non-waiting behavior being contrasted. Blocking receive waits for a message, blocking send waits for its defined completion condition, and direct message concerns how endpoints are addressed rather than whether the call blocks.

Evidence in the complete study note

Blocking send waits for the required handoff/buffering/receipt condition defined by its API. Blocking receive waits for a message. Nonblocking operations return promptly with data, progress or a would-block result. “Sender blocked until received” identifies blocking send under rendezvous semantics.OS09
Direct message communication names the receiving process explicitly. Indirect communication uses a mailbox or port. This naming distinction is independent of whether a send or receive blocks: naming a destination does not determine synchronization semantics.OS-S04

Choice by choice

A · correct — Nonblocking receive returns promptly instead of waiting until a message arrives, matching this asynchronous contrast.

B · incorrect — Blocking receive waits for a message rather than providing the non-waiting behavior requested.

C · incorrect — Blocking send waits for its defined handoff or receipt condition rather than returning promptly.

D · incorrect — Direct messaging describes endpoint addressing, not whether sending or receiving blocks.

Question 112

Which one of the following is NOT correct about threads?

  • A. Threads are the entities scheduled for execution on the CPU
  • B. It is a group of processes
  • C. Threads have program counters
  • D. Threads have registers to hold their working memory

Sources

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · Question 3-3

  • National Computer Science Exit Exam · Question 84

  • National Computer Science Exit Exam

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice collection · Question 213

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Compare per-thread execution context with shared process resources and the stated thread mapping.

Study-guide sections

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS08 · Threads and mapping models

Correct answer: B — It is a group of processes

PDF p27 defines a thread as a flow within one process, with its own PC and registers; p29 says kernel scheduling occurs on a thread basis. B incorrectly inverts containment by calling a thread a group of processes.

Evidence in the complete study note

A thread has its own PC, registers and stack. Threads in a process share code, address space and usually open-file resources. A process can contain many threads; a thread belongs to one process at a time. Threads are not a group of processes and need not execute sequentially. Independent processes can exist; cooperating ones communicate/share data.OS08
User-level threads are managed by a library; kernel threads are known to the OS. In many-to-one mapping, a blocking kernel call can block all user threads and one kernel execution context cannot use multiple CPUs simultaneously. One-to-one permits another kernel thread to run when one blocks. Many-to-many multiplexes user threads over several kernel threads.OS08

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Thread-basis scheduling is explicit on p29.

B · correct — Each thread belongs to exactly one process, not a collection of processes (p27).

C · incorrect — Own program counter is explicit (p27).

D · incorrect — Own system registers are explicit (p27); working memory should mean saved execution values rather than all shared process memory.

Question 113

A computer expert would like to know the number of processes that are completed per hour to assess the performance of a machine. Which one of the following best describes the expert's assessment?

  • A. Waiting time
  • B. System throughput
  • C. Turnaround time
  • D. Response time

Sources

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · Question 9-1

  • National Computer Science Exit Exam · Question 89

  • National Computer Science Exit Exam

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice collection · Question 214

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Construct a timeline and distinguish completion rates from individual elapsed-time measures.

Study-guide sections

OS13 · Scheduling metrics and calculation method

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS13 · Scheduling metrics and calculation method

Correct answer: B — System throughput.

OS13 defines throughput as completed jobs divided by elapsed time. Counting process completions per hour is exactly that rate. Waiting, turnaround and response are delays associated with an individual process or an average of such delays; they are not completion counts per hour. The stated measurement therefore identifies system throughput without any scheduling trace.

Evidence in the complete study note

For arrival A, first start S and completion C, response=S−A and turnaround=C−A. For a single CPU burst B with no I/O, waiting=C−A−B. With I/O, subtract all CPU and blocked time to isolate ready-queue waiting. Throughput=completed jobs/elapsed time; CPU utilization=busy time/elapsed time. Minimize response/turnaround/waiting under the selected goal and maximize useful throughput/utilization.OS13

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Waiting time measures ready-queue delay, not the number of processes completed in an hour.

B · correct — Completed processes divided by hours elapsed is system throughput here’s definition.

C · incorrect — Turnaround measures arrival-to-completion duration for a process rather than a system completion rate.

D · incorrect — Response measures arrival-to-first-start delay, not the number of jobs finished per unit time.

Question 114

A process is terminated by the operating system due to a reference to non-existing memory. Which one of the following best explains the process termination?

  • A. Normal exit
  • B. Error exit
  • C. Terminated by another process
  • D. Fatal exit

Sources

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam · Question 21-3

  • National Computer Science Exit Exam · Question 1

  • National Computer Science Exit Exam

  • MOE Computer Science Exit Exam

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice collection · Question 216

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Separate creation, image replacement and termination; classify termination by its cause.

Study-guide sections

OS07 · Creation, fork/exec and termination

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS07 · Creation, fork/exec and termination

Correct answer: D — Fatal exit.

OS07 explicitly identifies a reference to nonexistent/protected memory as a fatal fault. Here the OS terminates the process because of that fault, so this is involuntary fatal termination. It is not the program’s voluntary error exit, normal completion or a kill request by another ordinary process. The cause in the stem resolves the otherwise loose exit terminology.

Evidence in the complete study note

Normal completion and a program-detected error exit are voluntary; an unrecoverable fault or being killed is involuntary. exit() terminates normally through the runtime; abort() requests abnormal termination. A reference to nonexistent/protected memory is a fatal fault, unlike an application deciding to exit after bad input.OS07

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Normal exit is voluntary completion, unlike OS-enforced termination for an invalid memory reference.

B · incorrect — A program-detected error exit is voluntary; the stem specifies an unrecoverable memory-access fault.

C · incorrect — The stated cause is a fault handled by the OS, not a kill request from another process.

D · correct — The note explicitly classifies nonexistent-memory access as a fatal fault causing involuntary termination.

Question 115

What is a characteristic feature of round-robin scheduling?

  • A. It assigns priorities to tasks based on their relative importance
  • B. It always selects the task with the shortest remaining time
  • C. It favors short tasks over long tasks
  • D. It guarantees fairness by providing each task with an equal time slice

Sources

  • Computer Science Exit Exam · Sene 2016 · Question 2-3

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice collection · Question 226

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace bounded turns in ready-queue order and include the stated overhead and arrivals.

Study-guide sections

OS18 · Round Robin, quantum and bounded turns

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

OS16 · SJF/SJN and shortest remaining time

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS18 · Round Robin, quantum and bounded turns

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

OS16 · SJF/SJN and shortest remaining time

Correct answer: D — It guarantees fairness by providing each task with an equal time slice

PDF p25 gives each process a fixed quantum and preempts after it. D identifies that equal-turn mechanism; it guarantees equal-sized opportunities, not identical lifetime CPU totals for unequal jobs.

Evidence in the complete study note

RR serves a ready queue cyclically for at most quantum q per turn. Unfinished jobs return to the tail; completed/blocked jobs leave.OS18
Priority scheduling chooses the highest-priority ready job.OS17
Non-preemptive SJF/SJN chooses the shortest next burst among currently ready jobs. SRTF reconsiders on arrivals and selects the shortest remaining work; a shorter newcomer can preempt.OS16

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Priority rank is the p24 policy, not the RR criterion.

B · incorrect — Remaining-time selection is not RR’s fixed quantum.

C · incorrect — Short-job preference is not RR’s fixed quantum rule.

D · correct — Equal quantum per turn is precisely the p25 definition.

Question 117

All of the following are scheduling criteria EXCEPT:

  • A. CPU utilization
  • B. Min Turnaround Time
  • C. Max Throughput
  • D. Max Response Time

Construct a timeline and distinguish completion rates from individual elapsed-time measures.

Study-guide sections

OS13 · Scheduling metrics and calculation method

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS13 · Scheduling metrics and calculation method

Correct answer: D — Maximizing response time is the wrong optimization direction.

OS13 says to minimize response, turnaround and waiting while maximizing useful throughput/utilization under the chosen objective. Thus maximum response time is the mismatched criterion: making first response later worsens responsiveness. The other alternatives name a standard metric or its conventional optimization direction. The question is about preferred direction, not whether response time itself is measurable.

Evidence in the complete study note

For arrival A, first start S and completion C, response=S−A and turnaround=C−A. For a single CPU burst B with no I/O, waiting=C−A−B. With I/O, subtract all CPU and blocked time to isolate ready-queue waiting. Throughput=completed jobs/elapsed time; CPU utilization=busy time/elapsed time. Minimize response/turnaround/waiting under the selected goal and maximize useful throughput/utilization.OS13

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — CPU utilization is a standard scheduling metric measuring the fraction of elapsed time the CPU is busy.

B · incorrect — Minimizing turnaround is the conventional goal for reducing arrival-to-completion delay.

C · incorrect — Maximizing useful throughput is a conventional goal for increasing completed work per unit time.

D · correct — Response delay should ordinarily be minimized; maximizing it reverses the responsiveness objective.

Question 118

Which umbrella function of modern operating systems allocates and coordinates hardware resources such as CPU time, memory and I/O devices?

  • A. File Management
  • B. Resource Management
  • C. Process Management
  • D. Security Management

Separate broad resource-management goals from individual services or applications.

Study-guide sections

OS01 · Objectives, resource management and evolution

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

OS36 · Files, descriptors and ordinary operations

OS39 · Protection, authentication and threats

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS01 · Objectives, resource management and evolution

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

OS36 · Files, descriptors and ordinary operations

OS39 · Protection, authentication and threats

Correct answer: B — Resource Management

An OS allocates CPU time, memory, storage, devices and communication resources. The umbrella term for coordinating these allocations is resource management. File, process and security management cover narrower responsibilities, so none alone names the full scope of the question. Replacing device management with the umbrella category preserves the combined CPU/memory/I/O concept and gives the item one complete answer.

Evidence in the complete study note

An OS allocates CPU time, memory, storage, devices and communication resources, and provides services to programs.OS01
Long-term scheduling admits jobs; medium-term scheduling suspends/swaps them; short-term scheduling selects ready work.OS06
A file has content and metadata such as name, size, owner, permissions and timestamps. open obtains an open-file reference, read/write transfer bytes, seek changes an offset, close releases a reference, truncate changes size and unlink removes a name.OS36
Authentication verifies identity; authorization checks permitted operations.OS39

B · correct — Resource management spans CPU time, memory and I/O devices.

C · incorrect — Process management covers execution and CPU scheduling but is narrower than the combined scope.

D · incorrect — Security management controls protection and permissions rather than naming all allocation functions.

Question 119

Which scheduling algorithm is commonly associated with the concept of aging, where the priority of a task increases the longer it waits in the ready queue?

  • A. Round Robin (RR)
  • B. Shortest Job Next (SJN)
  • C. Priority Scheduling
  • D. Shortest Remaining Time First (SRTF)

Sources

  • Computer Science Exit Exam · Sene 2016 · Question 22-2

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice collection · Question 222

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Check priority direction, preemption and whether waiting can change effective rank.

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

OS18 · Round Robin, quantum and bounded turns

OS16 · SJF/SJN and shortest remaining time

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS17 · Priority, starvation and aging

OS18 · Round Robin, quantum and bounded turns

OS16 · SJF/SJN and shortest remaining time

Correct answer: C — Priority scheduling.

OS17 describes aging as increasing waiting jobs’ effective priority to reduce indefinite postponement. That is a direct adjustment within a priority scheme and matches the stem exactly. RR rotates turns, SJN selects the shortest next burst, and SRTF selects the shortest remaining work. Those policies may be modified, but they are not the named priority-aging mechanism requested.

Evidence in the complete study note

Aging increases waiting jobs’ effective priority according to a rule, reducing indefinite postponement when the priority scheme and workload permit.OS17
RR serves a ready queue cyclically for at most quantum q per turn.OS18
Non-preemptive SJF/SJN chooses the shortest next burst among currently ready jobs. SRTF reconsiders on arrivals and selects the shortest remaining work; a shorter newcomer can preempt.OS16

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — RR cycles ready jobs by quantum rather than defining aging through increasing priority.

B · incorrect — SJN chooses shortest next bursts; aging refers specifically to adjusting the effective priority of waiting work.

C · correct — Priority scheduling can raise a waiting task’s effective priority through aging to reduce starvation.

D · incorrect — SRTF compares remaining bursts rather than increasing priority solely because a task has waited longer.

Question 120

In a system susceptible to deadlock, what does the 'no preemption' condition imply?

  • A. Resources can be preempted and assigned to other processes
  • B. Processes cannot request additional resources while holding others
  • C. Resources cannot be preempted while they are in use
  • D. Processes must release all resources when blocked

Sources

  • Computer Science Exit Exam · Sene 2016 · Question 25-3

  • Computer Science Exit Exam practice collection · Question 227

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace the resource dependencies and distinguish necessary conditions from evidence of an actual deadlock.

Study-guide sections

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

OS24 · Prevention: identify the condition removed

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS22 · Deadlock and its four necessary conditions

OS24 · Prevention: identify the condition removed

Correct answer: C — Allocated resources cannot be forcibly withdrawn.

OS22 defines no resource preemption as requiring voluntary release by the holder. C states that resources cannot be preempted while in use and therefore matches. A reverses the condition. B and D describe policies attacking hold-and-wait by limiting requests or requiring release; they are different from the no-preemption property itself.

Evidence in the complete study note

Mutual exclusion allows at most one holder of a nonshareable resource. Hold-and-wait means retaining a resource while requesting another. No resource preemption means release must be voluntary. Circular wait forms a closed chain of such waits.OS22
“If waiting, release all held resources and try again” removes simultaneous holding while waiting.OS24

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Permitting withdrawal and reassignment is resource preemption, directly reversing the condition requested.

B · incorrect — Forbidding additional requests while holding resources attacks hold-and-wait rather than defining no-preemption.

C · correct — The holder retains the resource until voluntary release, so it cannot simply be forcibly withdrawn.

D · incorrect — Requiring release when blocked removes holding-while-waiting; it is not the no-preemption definition.

Question 121

When a process is in a "Blocked" state waiting for some Input-Output services and afterthe service is completed, it will goes to a state known as:

  • A. Terminated
  • B. Suspended
  • C. Ready
  • D. Running

Source

  • Computer Science model exam collection · Question 13

Decide whether the process is eligible, running or waiting, then identify the responsible queue or scheduling role.

Study-guide sections

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS06 · Process states, queues and schedulers

Correct answer: C — Ready

After I/O completion the process no longer waits for that event but must obtain CPU allocation, so it becomes Ready (PDF pp16,19–20). Running follows only when the scheduler actually selects it.

Evidence in the complete study note

New processes are created/admitted; ready means eligible for CPU but not currently running. Running uses a CPU. Waiting/blocked means unable to proceed until an event; terminated means execution has ended. Running→ready follows time-slice expiry or another preemption. Running→waiting follows blocking I/O. I/O completion normally gives waiting→ready, not a guaranteed immediate running state.OS06
Long-term scheduling admits jobs; medium-term scheduling suspends/swaps them; short-term scheduling selects ready work.OS06

Choice by choice

A · incorrect — Completion of an I/O request is not completion of the process.

B · incorrect — Suspension concerns memory removal/swapping, not an inevitable I/O-completion transition.

C · correct — Ready means eligible and waiting for processor assignment (p16).

D · incorrect — It can run after scheduling; I/O completion alone does not allocate CPU (p20).

Question 122

A memory management policy that decisions must be made as to which page or pages are to be supplanted when a memory is full is known as:

  • A. Cleaning policy
  • B. Fetch policy
  • C. Load policy
  • D. Replacement policy

Source

  • Computer Science model exam collection · Question 14

Hint / Module Reference (Where to Read)

Trace every reference, distinguish hits from faults and update the policy state consistently.

OS33 · Replacement-policy traces

OS-S03 · Memory allocation and page-policy decisions

Answer & Detailed Explanation

Study-guide references

OS33 · Replacement-policy traces

OS-S03 · Memory allocation and page-policy decisions

Correct answer: D — Replacement policy

Page replacement chooses which memory pages to swap out when allocation requires unavailable free space (PDF p40). D names that victim-selection policy.

Evidence in the complete study note

Replacement chooses a victim when no suitable free frame is available.OS33
A fetch policy decides when contents are brought into memory; demand paging fetches a valid nonresident page when it is accessed. A cleaning policy concerns writing modified contents to backing storage. A replacement policy chooses which resident page to evict. These decisions can cooperate during one page fault, but they are different decisions.OS-S03

C · incorrect — Loading concerns admission/bringing in work, rather than selecting pages to evict.

D · correct — Replacement is explicitly the decision of which pages to swap out (p40).

Which of the following is not a principal event that cause processes to be created?

OS07 · Creation, fork/exec and termination
